1	//! `vc-x1 clone` — clone a repo (URL or local path) into a workspace.
2	//!
3	//! - Default (no `--por`): dual-repo layout — clones code, derives
4	//!   bot source (`<source>.claude`), clones bot into
5	//!   `<target>/.claude`, creates the Claude Code symlink. Both
6	//!   sides must succeed.
7	//! - `--por`: single repo into `<target>`. No `.claude/`, no
8	//!   symlink.
9	//!
10	//! TARGET shapes (all routed through `parse_target`): URL,
11	//! `owner/name` shorthand, or a local path (`./X`, `/X`, `~/X`,
12	//! `.`, `..`). Path-form is symmetric with `git clone /local/bare.git`
13	//! — useful for fixtures and CI scratch dirs.
14	//!
15	//! `clone_one` and `clone_dual` are `pub(crate)` so init's `-3`
16	//! reshape can reuse them for the "URL exists → clone" preflight
17	//! path (per `notes/chores/chores-08.md > init + clone redesign`).
18	
19	use std::path::Path;
20	
21	use clap::Args;
22	use log::info;
23	
24	use crate::common::run;
25	use crate::context::Context;
26	use crate::options_flags::por::PorFlag;
27	use crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;
28	use crate::symlink;
29	use crate::url::{Target, derive_name, derive_session_url, parse_target, resolve_url};
30	
31	/// CLI args for `vc-x1 clone`.
32	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
33	pub struct CloneArgs {
34	    /// Source to clone — URL, owner/name shorthand, or local path.
35	    ///
36	    /// - URL: `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`, `https://...(.git)?`
37	    /// - owner/name shorthand: resolves to
38	    ///   `git@github.com:owner/name.git`
39	    /// - Local path: `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`, `.`, `..`
40	    ///   (passed directly to `git clone`)
41	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET", verbatim_doc_comment)]
42	    pub target: String,
43	
44	    /// Destination dir name in cwd [default: derived from TARGET]
45	    #[arg(value_name = "NAME")]
46	    pub name: Option<String>,
47	
48	    /// Flatten of the shared [`PorFlag`] leaf — `--por` switches
49	    /// the clone shape from dual (default) to single repo.
50	    #[command(flatten)]
51	    pub por: PorFlag,
52	
53	    /// Dry run — show what would be done without executing.
54	    #[arg(long)]
55	    pub dry_run: bool,
56	}
57	
58	/// Inputs to the clone op, flat, owned, clap-free.
59	///
60	/// - `target`: the `TARGET` positional (URL, `owner/name`, or
61	///   local path).
62	/// - `name`: optional `NAME` positional override for the
63	///   destination dir.
64	/// - `por`: `--por` resolved — `true` for plain single repo,
65	///   `false` (default) for dual.
66	/// - `dry_run`: `--dry-run`.
67	pub struct CloneParams {
68	    pub target: String,
69	    pub name: Option<String>,
70	    pub por: bool,
71	    pub dry_run: bool,
72	}
73	
74	impl From<&CloneArgs> for CloneParams {
75	    /// Convert clap-derived `CloneArgs` into the flat `CloneParams`
76	    /// (total — every field copies straight over).
77	    fn from(a: &CloneArgs) -> Self {
78	        Self {
79	            target: a.target.clone(),
80	            name: a.name.clone(),
81	            por: a.por.value,
82	            dry_run: a.dry_run,
83	        }
84	    }
85	}
86	
87	impl SubcommandRunner for CloneArgs {
88	    type Params = CloneParams;
89	
90	    /// Delegate to the existing `From<&CloneArgs>` impl above
91	    /// (total — never fails).
92	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {
93	        Ok(CloneParams::from(self))
94	    }
95	
96	    /// Run the existing `clone_repo` op.
97	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
98	        clone_repo(ctx, params)
99	    }
100	}
101	
102	/// Top-level clone driver.
103	///
104	/// - Resolves TARGET to a concrete clone source (URL or path).
105	/// - Determines destination dir name (`[NAME]` override or
106	///   `derive_name(TARGET)`).
107	/// - Pre-checks target dir doesn't exist.
108	/// - Dispatches to `clone_one` (POR) or `clone_dual` (code,bot).
109	///
110	/// `ctx` is unused today (clone reads neither user config nor the
111	/// `--log` path); it's present for the uniform subcommand-layer
112	/// signature.
113	pub fn clone_repo(_ctx: &Context, params: &CloneParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
114	    log::debug!("clone: enter");
115	
116	    let parsed = parse_target(&params.target)?;
117	    let source = match parsed {
118	        Target::Url(u) => u,
119	        Target::OwnerName(o, n) => resolve_url(&format!("{o}/{n}")),
120	        Target::Path(p) => p.to_str().ok_or("path is not valid UTF-8")?.to_string(),
121	        Target::BareName(n) => {
122	            return Err(format!(
123	                "'{n}' is a bare name — clone has no config-driven defaults; \
124	                 use 'owner/{n}', a full URL, or './{n}' for a local path"
125	            )
126	            .into());
127	        }
128	    };
129	
130	    let name = match &params.name {
131	        Some(n) => n.clone(),
132	        None => derive_name(&params.target)?,
133	    };
134	    let parent_dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
135	    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&name);
136	
137	    if project_dir.exists() {
138	        return Err(format!("'{}' already exists", project_dir.display()).into());
139	    }
140	
141	    if params.dry_run {
142	        info!("Dry run — would execute:");
143	        if params.por {
144	            info!("  1. jj git clone --colocate {source} {name}");
145	        } else {
146	            let session_source = derive_session_url(&source);
147	            info!("  1. jj git clone --colocate {source} {name}");
148	            info!("  2. jj git clone --colocate {session_source} {name}/.claude");
149	            info!("  3. Create Claude Code symlink");
150	        }
151	        return Ok(());
152	    }
153	
154	    run("jj", &["--version"], Path::new(".")).map_err(|_| "jj is not installed")?;
155	
156	    if params.por {
157	        clone_one(&source, &project_dir, &parent_dir)?;
158	        info!("");
159	        info!("Done! Project cloned to {}", project_dir.display());
160	        info!("  Code repo: {}", project_dir.display());
161	    } else {
162	        clone_dual(&source, &project_dir, &parent_dir)?;
163	    }
164	
165	    log::debug!("clone: exit");
166	    Ok(())
167	}
168	
169	/// Clone a single repo via `jj git clone --colocate` and verify
170	/// bookmark tracking.
171	///
172	/// - `source` is the clone source (URL or local path).
173	/// - `target_dir` is the destination working repo location.
174	/// - `parent_dir` is the cwd for the `jj git clone` subprocess.
175	///
176	/// `jj git clone --colocate` does git clone + jj init + automatic
177	/// bookmark tracking in one step (unlike colocate-after-bare-git-clone,
178	/// which needs explicit `jj bookmark track`). The `verify_tracking`
179	/// call asserts the post-clone state matches expectations.
180	pub(crate) fn clone_one(
181	    source: &str,
182	    target_dir: &Path,
183	    parent_dir: &Path,
184	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
185	    let target_str = target_dir
186	        .to_str()
187	        .ok_or("target path is not valid UTF-8")?;
188	    info!("Cloning {source} → {target_str}...");
189	    run(
190	        "jj",
191	        &["git", "clone", "--colocate", source, target_str],
192	        parent_dir,
193	    )?;
194	    crate::common::verify_tracking(target_dir, "main")?;
195	    Ok(())
196	}
197	
198	/// Orchestrate a dual-repo clone: code via `clone_one`, bot via
199	/// `clone_one` (no graceful skip — both sides required by the
200	/// default dual shape; users who want code-only pass `--por`),
201	/// then create the Claude Code symlink.
202	pub(crate) fn clone_dual(
203	    code_source: &str,
204	    target_dir: &Path,
205	    parent_dir: &Path,
206	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
207	    let session_source = derive_session_url(code_source);
208	    let session_dir = target_dir.join(".claude");
209	
210	    clone_one(code_source, target_dir, parent_dir)?;
211	    clone_one(&session_source, &session_dir, target_dir)?;
212	    info!("Creating Claude Code symlink...");
213	    let sl = symlink::install(target_dir)?;
214	
215	    info!("");
216	    info!("Done! Project cloned to {}", target_dir.display());
217	    info!("  Code repo:    {}", target_dir.display());
218	    info!("  Session repo: {}", session_dir.display());
219	    info!(
220	        "  Symlink:      {} -> {}",
221	        sl.symlink_path.display(),
222	        sl.abs_target.display()
223	    );
224	
225	    Ok(())
226	}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/tests/cli_sync.rs",
  "content": "//! CLI subprocess test for `vc-x1 sync` — the \"two machines\" flow.\n//!\n//! Drives the whole scenario through the binary: `vc-x1 init` a\n//! project with local bare remotes, `vc-x1 clone` it twice (trA /\n//! trB), change + `vc-x1 push` on trA, then `vc-x1 sync` on trB and\n//! verify trB's `main` moved to the pushed head. Raw `jj` is used\n//! only to inspect results, never to mutate.\n//!\n//! Two init/clone incompatibilities are worked around inline (both\n//! candidates for real fixes, tracked in notes):\n//!\n//! - init's local bare remotes leave HEAD at `refs/heads/master`\n//!   while the pushed branch is `main`, so a later `jj git clone`\n//!   has no default branch to auto-track and `vc-x1 clone` errors\n//!   → point HEAD at `main` with `git symbolic-ref`.\n//! - init names the session remote `remote-claude.git`, but clone\n//!   derives `<code-source-stem>.claude.git` → symlink\n//!   `remote-code.claude.git` → `remote-claude.git`.\n\nmod common;\n\nuse std::fs;\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse std::process::Command;\n\nuse common::{CliFixture, run_ok};\n\n/// Directory holding the `vc-x1` binary under test, for prepending\n/// to `PATH` so subcommands that re-invoke `vc-x1` (push preflight,\n/// finalize) find the same binary.\nfn bin_dir() -> PathBuf {\n    Path::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\"))\n        .parent()\n        .expect(\"binary has a parent dir\")\n        .to_path_buf()\n}\n\n/// `PATH` value with the test binary's dir prepended.\nfn test_path() -> String {\n    let orig = std::env::var(\"PATH\").unwrap_or_default();\n    format!(\"{}:{orig}\", bin_dir().display())\n}\n\n/// Run `jj <args>` in `dir` (inspection only) and assert success;\n/// returns trimmed stdout.\nfn jj(dir: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> String {\n    let out = Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args(args)\n        .current_dir(dir)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"spawn jj\");\n    assert!(\n        out.status.success(),\n        \"jj {args:?} failed in {}: {}\",\n        dir.display(),\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)\n    );\n    String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string()\n}\n\n/// Resolve `rev` to its short commit id in `repo`.\nfn cid(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> String {\n    jj(\n        repo,\n        &[\"log\", \"-r\", rev, \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"commit_id.short(12)\"],\n    )\n}\n\n/// Write a minimal jj user config under the fixture's isolated HOME\n/// so jj invocations spawned inside `vc-x1` have an identity.\nfn write_jj_config(home: &Path) {\n    let dir = home.join(\".config/jj\");\n    fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect(\"mkdir jj config dir\");\n    fs::write(\n        dir.join(\"config.toml\"),\n        \"[user]\\nname = \\\"cli-test\\\"\\nemail = \\\"cli-test@example.com\\\"\\n\",\n    )\n    .expect(\"write jj config\");\n}\n\n/// Point a bare remote's HEAD at `main` (init leaves it at\n/// `refs/heads/master` — see module docs).\nfn set_head_main(bare: &Path) {\n    let out = Command::new(\"git\")\n        .args([\"symbolic-ref\", \"HEAD\", \"refs/heads/main\"])\n        .current_dir(bare)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"spawn git symbolic-ref\");\n    assert!(\n        out.status.success(),\n        \"git symbolic-ref failed in {}: {}\",\n        bare.display(),\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)\n    );\n}\n\n/// Two `vc-x1 clone`s of one `vc-x1 init`ed project: trA pushes a\n/// change with `vc-x1 push`; `vc-x1 sync` on trB must move trB's\n/// `main` to the pushed head and reposition `@` onto it.\n#[test]\nfn cli_sync_moves_main_after_peer_push() {\n    let fx = CliFixture::new(\"sync-peer-push\");\n    write_jj_config(&fx.home);\n\n    // vc-x1 init ./tr --repo local=<base>\n    run_ok(\n        fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&fx.base)\n            .args([\"init\", \"./tr\"])\n            .arg(\"--repo\")\n            .arg(format!(\"local={}\", fx.base.display())),\n    );\n\n    // Workarounds — see module docs.\n    set_head_main(&fx.path(\"remote-code.git\"));\n    set_head_main(&fx.path(\"remote-claude.git\"));\n    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(\n        fx.path(\"remote-claude.git\"),\n        fx.path(\"remote-code.claude.git\"),\n    )\n    .expect(\"symlink session remote\");\n\n    // vc-x1 clone <code-remote> trB / trA (B first, so its\n    // main@origin is the pre-push head). Absolute source path —\n    // the derived session source is resolved from the clone's\n    // target dir, so a relative path would not resolve.\n    let code_remote = fx.path(\"remote-code.git\");\n    for name in [\"trB\", \"trA\"] {\n        run_ok(\n            fx.cmd()\n                .current_dir(&fx.base)\n                .arg(\"clone\")\n                .arg(&code_remote)\n                .arg(name),\n        );\n    }\n    let tr_a = fx.path(\"trA\");\n    let tr_b = fx.path(\"trB\");\n    let pre_main = cid(&tr_b, \"main\");\n\n    // Change + vc-x1 push on trA. `--from review` skips the cargo\n    // preflight (the fixture is not a Rust project); PATH carries\n    // the binary under test for push's internal `vc-x1` calls.\n    fs::write(tr_a.join(\"from-a.txt\"), \"from clone A\\n\").expect(\"write trA change\");\n    run_ok(\n        fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&tr_a)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .args([\n                \"push\",\n                \"main\",\n                \"--from\",\n                \"review\",\n                \"--title\",\n                \"feat: from clone A\",\n                \"--body\",\n                \"add from-a.txt\",\n                \"--yes\",\n            ]),\n    );\n    let pushed = cid(&tr_a, \"main\");\n    assert_ne!(pre_main, pushed, \"trA's push should advance the remote\");\n\n    // vc-x1 sync --no-check on trB.\n    run_ok(\n        fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&tr_b)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .args([\"sync\", \"--no-check\"]),\n    );\n\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(&tr_b, \"main\"),\n        pushed,\n        \"trB's main should move to trA's pushed head\"\n    );\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(&tr_b, \"@-\"),\n        pushed,\n        \"trB's @ should be repositioned onto the new main\"\n    );\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

All green — the full suite (392 tests) passes, including the new end-to-end CLI test.

**Bottom line: driven entirely through the binary — `vc-x1 init` → `vc-x1 clone` trA/trB → change + `vc-x1 push` on trA → `vc-x1 sync --no-check` on trB — the `main` bookmark in trB *does* move to trA's pushed head, and `@` is repositioned onto it.** So I still can't reproduce the failure you saw; the sync path itself is working.

The new test is `tests/cli_sync.rs` (`cli_sync_moves_main_after_peer_push`), alongside the earlier in-process test in `src/sync/integration_tests.rs`. Raw `jj` is used only to inspect results. Two notes on how the test drives `push`: it uses `--from review` (the fixture isn't a Rust project, so the cargo preflight can't run) and `--no-finalize` (finalize's detached form logs to a fixed `/tmp` path the sandbox blocks).

Building it surfaced **three real bugs**, worked around inline in the test (documented in its module docs):

1. **`init --repo local` leaves the bare remotes' HEAD at `refs/heads/master`** while the only branch is `main`. A later `jj git clone` therefore has no default branch to auto-track, and `vc-x1 clone` fails its `verify_tracking` check. Fix candidate: init should `git symbolic-ref HEAD refs/heads/main` in the bare repos.
2. **`clone`'s session-remote derivation doesn't match init's local naming**: clone derives `<code-source-stem>.claude.git` (`remote-code.claude.git`), but init creates `remote-claude.git`. Also, a relative clone TARGET breaks the session clone, because the derived session source is resolved from inside the just-cloned target dir.
3. **`vc-x1 push --body` starting with `- ` fails** — the internal `jj commit -m <body>` invocation rejects it with "unexpected argument '- '". Since project convention makes commit bodies bullet lists, this could bite real non-interactive pushes. (Plain `jj commit -m "- x"` works, so the misparse is somewhere in how push invokes it — not yet diagnosed.)

None of these explain "sync didn't move main", though bug 1 is adjacent: a repo whose `main` isn't tracking errors loudly rather than silently skipping. If you can share the command you ran and `jj log -r 'main | main@origin | @'` from the repo where main didn't move, I can chase the real case. Also happy to file the three bugs in `notes/bugs.md` and/or fix them in a cycle — say the word.

Nothing is committed; the two test files (plus the earlier `clone()` helper refactor in `src/sync/integration_tests.rs`) are sitting in the working copy.

> AGENT

Mystery solved — your transcript pins it. The bookmark actually *did* move; what didn't move is `@`, and the cause is that you ran `vc-x1 sync` in its default `--check` mode:

1. `jj git fetch` (which sync always runs, even in check mode) **auto-fast-forwards a tracked local `main`** to the fetched remote head. That's why after sync, `jj new main` landed you on `rzznvzyt "line 1"` — local `main` in t1B had already been updated by the fetch.
2. After the fetch, sync's classifier compares local `main` vs `main@origin`, finds them equal, and prints `sync: 2 repos, all bookmarks up-to-date` — technically true, because the fetch already did the fast-forward.
3. But the **reposition-`@` step (the 0.66.0 work) is gated on apply mode** — in `sync_repos` (src/sync.rs:295) it runs only `if params.no_check`. Default `vc-x1 sync` is `--check`, so the `jj new main` in both repos never happens and `@-` stays on the old commit (`Initial commit` in code, the root in `.claude`).

`vc-x1 sync --no-check` would have produced exactly the end state you showed. My tests passed because they all use apply mode — with a default-mode `vc-x1 sync` in the CLI test, it reproduces: main moves (fetch auto-ff) but `@` stays behind.

So the design gap is: check mode is documented as "fetch + report, no action", but the fetch already mutates `main` as a side effect — and then the repositioning that should follow a moved `main` is skipped. Two coherent ways to close it:

- **Reposition in check mode too** — treat "@ off the synced bookmark" like the fetch's auto-ff: part of keeping the workspace coherent, not an "action" in the behind/diverged sense. Check mode would still error on behind/diverged repos, but a clean fetch would end with `@` on the new main. This matches what you expected `vc-x1 sync` to do.
- **Report instead** — in check mode, detect that `@-` is no longer on the bookmark tip and say so ("main updated by fetch; run `vc-x1 sync --no-check` to reposition @"), keeping check mode mutation-free (beyond what jj's fetch already does).

The first matches your stated expectation ("the above is what the result should have been after `vc-x1 sync`"). Want me to implement repositioning in check mode (plus extend `tests/cli_sync.rs` to run default-mode sync and assert `@-` lands on the new main in both repos)?

But there's one real fork in the design before I open a cycle: what "apply" covers for a **diverged** repo, and whether `--check` survives for scripting (push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` today and relies on it not rewriting history mid-push).

> AGENT

Good news first: the "how to revert" half already exists — on any failure, sync snapshots every repo's jj operation id up front and automatically `jj op restore`s all of them, so a failed sync ends with "nothing changed" plus the error. The gap is only the default mode. My thoughts:

**Default becomes apply.** Plain `vc-x1 sync` = today's `--no-check`: fetch, fast-forward, rebase diverged, reposition `@` onto the synced bookmark. If a rebase produces conflicts, everything is auto-reverted and the error says so. That matches exactly what you expected in the t1B transcript.

**Scripts: keep `--check` as the opt-in verify-only mode.** That's the clean split:

- Scripts that want to *apply* just call `vc-x1 sync` — no flag needed, and the behavior can't drift under them because `--check` remains available to lock in the other contract.
- Scripts that must *not* mutate — push's preflight is the in-tree example, it runs `vc-x1 sync --check` specifically so a mid-push sync doesn't rewrite the history it's about to commit — keep passing `--check`.
- `--no-check` becomes redundant: keep it for one release as a hidden deprecated alias of the default (so nothing breaks), then remove.

One honest caveat to record with the change: `--check` was never fully read-only anyway — `jj git fetch` auto-fast-forwards a tracked `main` as a side effect (that's precisely what bit you). So `--check` really means "no vc-x1-initiated rewrites", and its help text should say that.

**On a revert command:** I'd hold off. Failure paths already self-revert. If you want to undo a *successful* sync, that's `jj op restore` territory; a future `vc-x1 sync --undo` (using the same op snapshots, printed at the end of each sync) is a nice todo entry rather than part of this change.

The cycle would be: flip the default in `SyncArgs`/`SyncParams`, keep `--check`, alias `--no-check`, update the doc comments and help, adapt the unit + integration tests, and extend `tests/cli_sync.rs` so the default-mode sync is the one that must move `main` *and* reposition `@` in both repos — the regression test for your exact scenario. Want me to open it?

> DEVELOPER

The problem is the remote could have changed between the two sync commands executing sync twice doesn't seem correct we should have a separate 'move-bookmark' or some such "apply" means nothing to me.

> AGENT

I follow now — the modes themselves are the design smell. Verify-then-apply as two *separate invocations* is inherently racy (the remote can move between them), so offering `--check`/`--no-check` bakes a race into the interface and forces script authors to pick a meaningless word. Let me restate the design I think you're describing:

- **`vc-x1 sync` — one command, no modes.** In a single run: fetch, classify, fast-forward/converge the bookmark, reposition `@`. If anything goes wrong (e.g. rebase conflicts), auto-revert everything and report. Verify-then-act happens *inside* one invocation against one fetch snapshot, so there's no window where the remote changes between "check" and "do". (The residual race — remote moves after our fetch — is guarded where it belongs: `jj git push` rejects a non-fast-forward at push time.)
- **The mutating-local-state piece gets its own honestly-named command** — `move-bookmark` (or `reposition`): move the bookmark / `@` onto a target without any fetch. That's the standalone tool for "main is right, my working copy isn't on it", and sync's final step becomes a call to the same logic.

Then push's preflight stops shelling out to `sync --check` entirely. Two options there, and this is the one open question:

1. **Push's first stage *is* a real sync** — atomic within the push run. If sync errors, push stops before committing anything; if it fast-forwards/repositions, that's exactly the state you want to be pushing from. No read-only mode needed anywhere.
2. Push does a read-only classification internally (library call, not a subcommand) and refuses if behind/diverged, telling you to run `vc-x1 sync` first. Keeps push from ever moving your working copy, but reintroduces a two-invocation gap between that check and your manual sync.

I'd go with option 1 — it's the same "one atomic run" principle applied to push. Does that match what you have in mind? If so I'll open a cycle: collapse sync's modes (drop `--check`/`--no-check`), extract `move-bookmark`, rewire push preflight, and make the CLI test assert your t1A/t1B scenario end-to-end with plain `vc-x1 sync`.

> DEVELOPER

I'm not sure we should automatically revert then the user can't inspect what happened. The app should stop on an error do not revert but indicate what the problem was. And then there should be a separate command which does the revert.

> AGENT

Agreed — that's the better principle: an error should leave the evidence in place. jj makes this safe, since every mutation is in `jj op log` and nothing is lost by stopping. So the design settles as:

- **`vc-x1 sync`** — one command, no modes. Fetch → converge bookmark → reposition `@`, all in one run. On error: **stop where it failed**, leave state untouched for inspection, and report what failed plus exactly how to undo (repos + their pre-sync op ids).
- **Snapshot instead of rollback.** Sync already records each repo's operation id before acting; instead of consuming those for an auto-restore, it persists them (a small state file, like push's saved state) and prints them on failure.
- **`vc-x1 revert`** (new command) — restores the repos to the persisted pre-sync snapshot via `jj op restore`, then clears the state. Manual, explicit, run only after you've looked at what happened.
- **`vc-x1 move-bookmark`** — the standalone "put the bookmark / `@` where it belongs" piece, no fetch; sync's last step reuses it.
- **Push preflight** becomes a real in-process sync (stop-on-error applies there too — a failed sync stops the push before anything is committed), no more `sync --check` shell-out, and the `--check`/`--no-check` flags go away.

One consequence to be aware of: push currently auto-rolls-back its commit stages on failure. Under this philosophy that should eventually become stop + `vc-x1 revert` too, but I'd keep this effort scoped to sync and take push's rollback as a follow-up todo.

This is more than one cycle of work. I'd sequence it: (1) sync single-mode + stop-on-error + persisted snapshot + `revert`, with the CLI two-clone test asserting your t1A/t1B scenario under plain `vc-x1 sync`; (2) `move-bookmark` extraction; (3) push preflight rewire + flag removal. 1	# Cycle protocol
2	
3	This protocol uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form).
4	
5	## Cycles
6	
7	A cycle has three phases:
8	
9	- **[Preparation](#preparation)** (`X.Y.Z-0`) — the cycle's
10	  first commit. Sets up the cycle:
11	  - Backfill the previous cycle's chores `Commits:` ref.
12	  - Bump `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
13	  - Pick up a `## Todo` item (typically the top-ranked,
14	    #1) into `## In Progress` (bold title + succinct problem
15	    statement + plan ladder).
16	  - Open the [chores section](#chores-sections).
17	- **[Work-N](#work-n)** (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …) — the
18	  commits that implement the change. As many as the change
19	  needs; each runs through the
20	  [per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow).
21	- **[Close-out](#close-out)** (bare `X.Y.Z`) — the cycle's
22	  last commit. Bookkeeping only:
23	  - Move the picked-up item to a one-line entry in
24	    `## Done`.
25	  - Move the `## In Progress` block into the
26	    [chores section](#chores-sections).
27	  - Optionally update `notes/README.md` if functionality
28	    changed.
29	
30	A cycle's commits are published to the project remote
31	either incrementally or as one batch at close-out; the
32	result must always be published at close-out. See
33	[Pushing](#pushing).
34	
35	**Sub-cycles.** When a Work commit's scope grows enough to
36	warrant its own ladder, it subdivides — `X.Y.Z-3.0`
37	Preparation, `X.Y.Z-3.1` / `X.Y.Z-3.2` Work, `X.Y.Z-3`
38	Close-out. The same three-phase shape applies recursively
39	at every depth. See [Numbering](#numbering) for the
40	suffix rule and [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)
41	for the local-ladder mechanics.
42	
43	## Chores sections
44	
45	A **chores section** is a `##` section in
46	`notes/chores/chores-NN.md` recording one cycle:
47	
48	- One section per cycle is the default.
49	- Per-commit sections (like 0.55.0's four) are a
50	  deliberate alternative.
51	
52	The phrase **"Open" the chores section** means append a
53	`##` header to the current `notes/chores/chores-NN.md`
54	with the cycle's anticipated close-out title (e.g.
55	`## refactor: foo bar (X.Y.Z)`). The body is empty;
56	content arrives at close-out (see [Close-out](#close-out)).
57	
58	Fuller chores conventions (content rules, header sync,
59	design subsection pattern, `Commits:` formatting) live in
60	AGENTS.md [Chores conventions](../AGENTS.md#chores-conventions).
61	
62	## Preparation
63	
64	The cycle's first commit (`X.Y.Z-0`):
65	
66	- **Backfill the previous cycle's chores section
67	  `Commits:` ref** — see
68	  [Chores commit references](../AGENTS.md#chores-commit-references).
69	- **Bump the version** in `Cargo.toml` to `X.Y.Z-0`.
70	- **Update `Cargo.lock`** to match — run `cargo build`
71	  (or `cargo check`) so the lockfile's `vc-x1` version
72	  tracks `Cargo.toml` in the same commit.
73	- **Move a `## Todo` item** (if the cycle has one) into
74	  `## In Progress` and the todo item should have:
75	  - A **bold title line** — that will be the chores
76	    section header, minus the `## ` prefix.
77	  - A **succinct problem statement**; add if one is needed
78	  - A **plan ladder**.
79	- **Open the [chores section](#chores-sections)** —
80	  append a `##` header with the cycle's anticipated
81	  close-out title.
82	
83	## Work-N
84	
85	The cycle's work commits (`X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, …)
86	implement the change. As many as needed:
87	
88	- Each commit runs through the
89	  **[per-commit flow](#per-commit-flow)**.
90	- **Interim pushes** are optional (backup, progress
91	  visibility).
92	- Close-out is the only mandatory push (see
93	  [Pushing](#pushing)).
94	- **Subdivide into a sub-cycle** if a Work commit's
95	  scope grows enough (see
96	  [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders)).
97	
98	## Close-out
99	
100	The cycle's last commit (bare `X.Y.Z`) does bookkeeping
101	only — the commit body describes that bookkeeping, not
102	what happens post-squash:
103	
104	- **Move the picked-up item** from `## In Progress` to a
105	  one-line entry (with a chores `[N]` ref) in `## Done`.
106	- **Move the `## In Progress` block into the
107	  [chores section](#chores-sections)** (the title-only
108	  placeholder opened during Preparation):
109	  - Cut the problem statement + plan ladder out of
110	    `## In Progress` (drop the bold title line — the
111	    chores `##` header already carries that string).
112	  - Paste under the chores `##` header.
113	  - Sync the chores header to the **final** commit title
114	    if the cycle's scope shifted; update every anchor
115	    back-reference.
116	  - Add an `### As-built ladder` listing the cycle's
117	    commits; optional `### Outcome` notes.
118	  - Replace `## In Progress` with
119	    `_No cycle currently in progress._`.
120	- **Update `notes/README.md`** if functionality changed
121	  (new flags, new subcommands, changed behavior).
122	
123	Whether to **squash** the cycle into one commit before the
124	publishing push, or push as-is, is decided at push time —
125	see [Pushing](#pushing).
126	
127	## Numbering
128	
129	The version suffix on each commit encodes its phase —
130	the **final identifier `0` marks a Preparation**:
131	
132	- `X.Y.Z-0` — Preparation
133	- `X.Y.Z-1`, `X.Y.Z-2`, … — Work commits
134	- `X.Y.Z` — Close-out (bare version, no suffix)
135	
136	Disambiguation:
137	
138	- `-10` — Work commit #10 (final identifier `10`), not a
139	  Preparation.
140	- `-1.0` — Preparation of the `-1` sub-cycle (final
141	  identifier `0`).
142	
143	**Nesting.** Sub-cycles append another level, recursively:
144	
145	- `X.Y.Z-3.0` — Preparation of the `-3` sub-cycle
146	- `X.Y.Z-3.1`, `X.Y.Z-3.2` — its Work
147	- `X.Y.Z-3` — its Close-out
148	- `X.Y.Z-3.1.0` — Preparation of the `-3.1` sub-sub-cycle
149	
150	Sub-cycles needing no Preparation omit `.0` (`-3.1`,
151	`-3.2`, `-3`); one that grows a Preparation later adds
152	`-3.0` without renumbering siblings.
153	
154	Bump `Cargo.toml` at the start of each phase so `vc-x1 -V`
155	reports the active phase at build time.
156	
157	## Per-commit flow
158	
159	Every commit (Preparation, each Work commit, Close-out) goes
160	through:
161	
162	1. **Mark this commit `(current)`** as the first edit in
163	   `notes/todo.md > ## In Progress`.
164	2. **Do the work** (see [Iterative work](#iterative-work)
165	   for the loop-and-squash technique).
166	3. **Flip this commit `(current)` → `(done)`** in `## In
167	   Progress` — before the cargo cycle and the commit.
168	4. **Cargo cycle** (skip-able for purely-docs commits; full
169	   cycle mandatory at close-out):
170	   1. `cargo fmt`
171	   2. `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`
172	   3. `cargo test`
173	   4. `cargo install --path . --locked`
174	   5. (re-test if anything substantive changed)
175	5. **Work review.** Stop *before* writing any description;
176	   tell the user "ready to commit." The user reviews the
177	   changes and we iterate until complete.
178	6. **Write the commit description** — see
179	   [Commit description](#commit-description).
180	7. **Commit Description review.** Show the title + body
181	   and stop. The user reviews the description. Iterate.
182	8. **Commit.** `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .` for
183	   the app repo, `-R .claude` for the bot repo (`-R` last
184	   keeps the verb visible):
185	
186	   ```
187	   jj commit -m \
188	   "<type>: <short description> (<version>)" \
189	   -m "<intro paragraph>
190	
191	   - file1: gist
192	   - file2: gist
193	
194	   ochid: /.claude/<chid>" \
195	   -R .
196	   ```
197	
198	**Two overrides apply:**
199	
200	- **Deviation or question** — any time the work deviates
201	  from the agreed plan, or a question arises, stop and
202	  surface it; don't push through.
203	- **ESC-ESC** — the user can interrupt at any point to pull
204	  a review or question forward.
205	
206	## Commit description
207	
208	[Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/)
209	with a version suffix:
210	
211	```
212	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
213	```
214	
215	### Title
216	
217	- ≤50 chars total (version suffix counts).
218	- Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `test`,
219	  `docs`, `chore`.
220	- Favor terse phrasings.
221	
222	### Body
223	
224	[Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form) (intro + bullets),
225	wrap ≤72. Bullet content differs per repo:
226	
227	- **App-repo body**: file-by-file. One bullet per file
228	  changed (file plus a one-line gist). Sub-bullets for
229	  files with multiple distinct changes:
230	
231	  ```
232	  - path/to/file1
233	    - first distinct change
234	    - second distinct change, wrapping to next line
235	       with continuation indented 5
236	  ```
237	
238	  The file-by-file list is the source of truth for the
239	  cycle's mechanical change record. Chores carries the
240	  narrative + design, not a copy of it. Promote any
241	  "why" beyond one sentence to a chores `###` subsection.
242	
243	- **Session-repo (`.claude`) body**: bullets describe
244	  in-session activity rather than code changes.
245	
246	### Trailer
247	
248	`ochid:` as the last line of the body — see
249	[ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers).
250	
251	## Reviewing changes
252	
253	Work review looks at the **uncommitted working-copy diff**,
254	on the way to commit. The user opens diffs in their
255	editor (Zed, VSCode); jj commands are for terminal:
256	
257	- `jj diff` — working-copy diff (uncommitted)
258	- `jj diff -r @-` — diff of the previous commit
259	- `jj diff --from <X> --to <Y>` — any two revisions
260	- `jj show -r <X>` — description + diff for one rev
261	
262	Don't `jj edit -r @-` to view a past commit — that marks
263	it mutable and shifts `@`; use `jj diff -r @-` or
264	`jj show -r @-`.
265	
266	See [Sub-cycle ladders](#sub-cycle-ladders) for the
267	close-out squash recipe and recovery; revset primitives
268	are in [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md).
269	
270	## Pushing
271	
272	### Policy
273	
274	Push is **discretionary** during the cycle (backup,
275	progress visibility) and **mandatory at close-out** —
276	the cycle's result must be published.
277	
278	### Shape at close-out push
279	
280	At close-out the cycle's *work* is done; its *published
281	shape* is the remaining choice, made at push time. Surface
282	the options and get user approval before pushing — once on
283	the target, changing shape is a remote rewrite (force-push,
284	needs approval), so choose deliberately.
285	
286	- **Squash to one commit** — single entry on the target.
287	  Right for straightforward changes where the Work-N is
288	  focused on one or two files.
289	- **Merge non-ff** *(current default)* — `main` gains a
290	  merge commit (`X.Y.Z`); cycle commits stay reachable via
291	  two parents. `jj log -r ..@ -n <N>` shows the trapezoidal
292	  shape. See [Merge non-ff recipe](#merge-non-ff-recipe)
293	  for the full setup sequence.
294	- **Keep separate** — one commit per cycle entry on
295	  `main`. Use when the decomposition itself is
296	  informative. Each chores section keeps its own header /
297	  `Commits:` ref; no consolidation churn.
298	
299	Set up squash/merge before invoking `vc-x1 push`; use
300	`jj git push` directly for non-standard shapes.
301	
302	### Merge non-ff recipe
303	
304	Setting up a [Merge non-ff](#shape-at-close-out-push)
305	close-out is a fixed sequence. `<closeout>` is the cycle's
306	close-out commit, `<prev>` the previous cycle's close-out
307	(the current `main` tip), `<work-tip>` the cycle's last
308	Work commit:
309	
310	1. **Rebase the close-out into a merge** — `jj rebase -r
311	   <closeout> --onto <prev> --onto <work-tip>`.
312	   - `-r <closeout>` keeps the `<closeout>` commit in place.
313	   - `--onto <prev>` becomes its first parent (trunk).
314	   - `--onto <work-tip>` becomes the second parent.
315	
316	   Together these make `<closeout>` a merge of
317	   `<prev>` + `<work-tip>`, forming a trapezoidal commit.
318	2. **Use `jj new <merge>`** to add an empty `@` above the
319	   merge. The rebase left `@` *on* the now-content-bearing
320	   merge, which git/IDE diff views show as uncommitted;
321	   `jj new` restores the clean empty `@` on top.
322	3. **Push** — `jj git push --bookmark main -R .`.
323	
324	**Post-hoc caveat.** If the cycle was already pushed
325	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) its commits are
326	immutable: the rebase needs `--ignore-immutable` and the
327	push force-updates `main`.
328	The standard sequence assumes the merge is set up *before*
329	the close-out push.
330	
331	### vc-x1 push wrapper
332	
333	`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps per-push mechanics. See
334	`vc-x1 push --help` for current flags.
335	
336	`vc-x1 push` injects the `ochid:` trailers itself (the
337	`commit-app` / `commit-claude` stages append them) — don't
338	hand-write them into the commit body or `--title`/`--body`.
339	
340	**Current limitation**: only fully supports the
341	[Keep separate](#shape-at-close-out-push) shape; other
342	shapes need manual jj steps. Improvements tracked /
343	planned:
344	
345	- N:1 code↔bot for Merge non-ff (`## Todo` entry P1).
346	- Symmetric squash (planned, to be captured in `-2`).
347	- Per-repo bookmark names (planned).
348	
349	### .claude cadence
350	
351	**Cadence**: one push = one `.claude` commit, paired
352	with every code commit in that push.
353	
354	The `.claude` working copy accumulates session data
355	across the cycle; its change ID stays stable across
356	snapshots, `jj describe`, and the finalize commit, so
357	code-side `ochid:` trailers resolve.
358	
359	`.claude` is a linear journal — all session work lives
360	on `main`, regardless of the app-side bookmark. **Do not
361	create or maintain `.claude` bookmarks that mirror
362	app-side branches** — risks the bot steering session
363	pushes to the wrong remote ref.
364	
365	Ending a session: if the user runs `/exit` there will be
366	session information created, which we don't worry about.
367	The user can close the terminal instead and `@` will
368	remain empty — this behavior was verified in 0.64.0.
369	
370	### Bot communication at the reviews
371	
372	Use plain prose — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal",
373	"Checkpoint N", etc.):
374	
375	- **At Work review** — summarize what changed and stop.
376	  "Work complete. Please review."
377	- **At Commit Description review** — present `$TITLE`
378	  and `$BODY` explicitly; ask permission to commit/push.
379	  Don't spell out the full `vc-x1 push ... --title ...
380	  --body ...` invocation by default.
381	- **At Post close-out review** — surface the shape
382	  options (squash / merge / keep) and the push target;
383	  wait for the user's choice before any `jj squash` /
384	  `jj rebase` / `jj git push` invocation.
385	
386	### After push or finalize: stop and wait
387	
388	After you **push** (cross the remote boundary) or launch
389	**finalize** on the `.claude` repo (`vc-x1 finalize --repo
390	.claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach`) — by
391	hand or via the `vc-x1 push` wrapper — you **MUST NEVER**
392	proceed (next step, edit, tool call, text output) until the
393	user explicitly directs you to continue. **Even when the next
394	step seems obvious — wait.** Treat push-or-finalize as a hard
395	stop for the whole turn.
396	
397	`vc-x1 push` performs both as its tail stages — `push-app`
398	then the detached `vc-x1 finalize` on `.claude` — so there is
399	no gap left to speak in once the push is invoked. Put **all**
400	closing words *before* invoking it; do not purposely emit anything
401	after the tool returns.
402	
403	Why (`.claude` is the live journal finalize snapshots →
404	squashes → pushes):
405	
406	- **`--detach`**: lets the tool return immediately so the
407	  harness can keep flushing the *trailing* session-data — the
408	  tool call's own transcript and any words emitted *before*
409	  the invoke — into the snapshot.
410	- **`--delay 10`**: gives the harness time to complete that
411	  flush before the snapshot is taken. It is **not** time for
412	  the bot to do more work.
413	- **silence**: the bot must not summarize or perform any work
414	  after the tool returns.
415	
416	**Known slip**: the bot has emitted a summary after launching
417	finalize. There is no "harmless" closing line — if it's worth
418	saying, say it *before* the invoke.
419	
420	### Recovery
421	
422	- **If push exits before `finalize-claude`** (e.g.
423	  failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`), run
424	  finalize by hand:
425	
426	  ```
427	  vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
428	  ```
429	
430	- **Run finalize again if `@` is non-empty** after the
431	  finalize's squash-and-commit (also desirable after
432	  extra activity by the bot's agents).
433	  - Why: finalize snapshots `.claude` at `--delay`, but the
434	    bot keeps writing session data while it runs — the tool's
435	    own invocation plus the bot's closing response land
436	    *after* the snapshot.
437	  - Safe to repeat: bot session data is append-only, so a
438	    re-run never conflicts or overwrites. (This could
439	    change; it is not under the user's control.)
440	  - Fix: run `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push
441	    <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach` another time to capture
442	    that tail; one extra pass is generally enough.
443	    - Verified 0.64.0: two finalizes were executed and
444	      we purposely used the bot again and the second finalize
445	      captured everything.
446	  - No guarantees: events outside the bot's control can leave
447	    `@` non-empty — e.g. the bot's back end may decide to
448	    squash/consolidate session data, which can take minutes
449	    and land after the snapshot. The remedy is the same: just
450	    run finalize again. This is why a single pass is never
451	    guaranteed to leave `@` empty.
452	- **Clear push's saved state** after any out-of-band
453	  recovery — `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml` or `vc-x1 push
454	  <bookmark> --restart` — otherwise push resumes from a
455	  stale stage.
456	- **Late code-repo tweak after `push-app` succeeded**
457	  (e.g. updating AGENTS.md or memory) requires `jj
458	  squash --ignore-immutable` and a re-push; that is a
459	  remote rewrite and needs explicit approval like any
460	  push.
461	
462	## ochid trailers
463	
464	A **chid** is jj's change ID — a permanent identifier
465	that survives rebases and describes (see
466	[`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md)). An **ochid** trailer
467	on a commit body links it to its counterpart in the
468	other repo via that counterpart's chid, written as a
469	workspace-root-relative path. See
470	[`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) for the
471	multi-line trailer design (multi-user / forking).
472	
473	Path semantics — paths start with `/`, the workspace root
474	(the app repo); `/.claude` is the bot session sub-repo:
475	
476	- `ochid: /<chid>` references a change in the **app repo**.
477	- `ochid: /.claude/<chid>` references a change in the
478	  **`.claude` repo**.
479	
480	Trailers are blank-line-separated `key: value` lines at the
481	end of the commit body, using the chid's **12-character**
482	prefix:
483	
484	```
485	ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqowktp   # points to a .claude change
486	ochid: /wtpmottvxqzl           # points to an app change
487	```
488	
489	How many, and which direction:
490	
491	- **Code-side commits** each carry one
492	  `ochid: /.claude/<.claude-chid>` — the `.claude`
493	  change ID.
494	- **The `.claude` commit** has one `ochid: /<code-chid>`
495	  per code commit in that push. More than one occurs on
496	  Merge non-ff close-out (one ochid per Work commit in
497	  the cycle).
498	
499	Use `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot -L` to capture the change
500	IDs (first line app, second `.claude`).
501	
502	### Resolvability
503	
504	A change ID travels with its commit: a **pushed** commit
505	resolves to the same chid in every clone — cloning the
506	`.claude` repo gave the published `main` tip the same chid
507	as an existing clone. The bot thinks jj carries the change
508	ID in the git commit object, so it survives
509	`jj git clone` / fetch.
510	
511	The local-only case is the **working-copy `@`**: jj mints a
512	fresh random chid for `@` in each clone, so an unpushed `@`
513	is never a stable ochid target. This is why a `.claude`
514	ochid names `@-` (the last committed change), not `@`.
515	
516	### .vc-config.toml
517	
518	Each repo contains a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its
519	location within the workspace, so tools resolve these paths
520	without repeating the workspace path in every trailer:
521	
522	```toml
523	# In vc-x1 (workspace root):
524	[workspace]
525	path = "/"
526	
527	# In .claude (sub-repo):
528	[workspace]
529	path = "/.claude"
530	```
531	
532	## Iterative work
533	
534	When work for a single commit (the **target**) benefits
535	from incremental review, loop:
536	
537	1. `jj new -R .` — fresh empty `@` on top of the target.
538	2. Make the next round of changes.
539	3. User reviews the round (see
540	   [Reviewing changes](#reviewing-changes)).
541	4. `jj squash -R .` folds into the target and creates a
542	   new empty `@`.
543	5. If not done, go to step 2.
544	
545	Same jj mechanics as a
546	[sub-cycle ladder](#sub-cycle-ladders), but at
547	single-commit scope — the cycle's version suffix
548	doesn't change.
549	
550	## Sub-cycle ladders
551	
552	When a Work commit subdivides into a sub-cycle (see
553	[Numbering](#numbering) for suffix nesting), its Work
554	commits typically live as a local jj `@` chain and
555	**collapse into the sub-cycle's Close-out** before the
556	parent cycle continues. Ladder commits are scratch —
557	for review and bisection only.
558	
559	### Per-Work-commit contract within a ladder
560	
561	For each Work commit in the ladder:
562	
563	1. `jj new -R .` — create a fresh empty `@`.
564	2. Do the commit's work.
565	3. Run `cargo test --bins`. **Non-negotiable** — build
566	   and clippy alone miss regressions until a later
567	   commit runs the full suite, raising bisection cost.
568	4. `jj describe -m "..." -m "..." -R .` — working title
569	   only (no version suffix); the sub-cycle Close-out
570	   collects everything into one final commit with the
571	   `(X.Y.Z-N)` marker.
572	
573	### Navigating the ladder
574	
575	Common moves:
576	
577	- `jj log -r '<base>::' -R .` — see the whole ladder
578	  from its base.
579	- `jj edit -r <prefix> -R .` — jump `@` to any ladder
580	  commit by chid prefix; useful for bisection.
581	- `jj edit @-- -R .` — quick-jump back two commits.
582	- `jj diff -r <chid> -R .` — review one commit in
583	  isolation.
584	
585	Modifications to any ladder commit rewrite it in place;
586	descendants auto-rebase.
587	
588	### Close-out: squash the ladder
589	
590	When all ladder Work commits are done and tests pass:
591	
592	```
593	jj squash --from "<base>..@-" --into @ -u -R .
594	```
595	
596	`<base>` is the parent of the first ladder commit; `-u`
597	keeps `@`'s description and discards the sources'.
598	After squash, history is linear: `<base> → @`;
599	intermediate commits are auto-abandoned.
600	
601	Then `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` as for any other commit.
602	
603	For N = 1 the squash is a no-op (`<base>..@-` is empty
604	when `@-` is `<base>`); push the single commit directly.
605	
606	### Recovery
607	
608	If a ladder commit goes wrong, back out without losing
609	prior commits:
610	
611	- **Discard the current commit.** `jj abandon @ -R .`
612	  drops it; you get a fresh empty `@` on the same
613	  parent.
614	- **Edit an earlier commit.** `jj edit -r <chid> -R .`,
615	  make corrections, then `jj edit -r <last-ladder-chid>`
616	  to return. Descendants auto-rebase.
617	- **Discard the entire ladder.** `jj op log -R .` shows
618	  the op history; `jj op restore <op-id> -R .` reverts
619	  to that point. Full undo — removes *all* ladder work
620	  after the chosen op. Use only to start over.
621	
622	# References
623	
624	- [`jj-revsets.md`](jj-revsets.md) — revset primitives
625	  (chid/cid, `@`/`@-`/`@+`, `..`/`::` ranges, prefix matching).
626	- [`forks-multi-user.md`](forks-multi-user.md) — multi-ochid
627	  trailer design (fork / multi-user generalization).
628	- [`substep-test.sh`](substep-test.sh) — script that
629	  scaffolds a 4-revision ladder under `/tmp/substep-test`
630	  for squash-recipe experiments.
631	- 0.41.1-6.5 cycle — first multi-commit ladder usage. The
632	  per-commit `cargo test --bins` gate originated there
633	  after a regression introduced in an early commit wasn't
634	  caught until a later one ran the full suite.
635	
```

35	1. **Version-number protocol is fragile — versions are
36	   baked into titles/bodies/todo/done/chores before the
37	   change lands.** The cycle protocol embeds an `X.Y.Z-N`
38	   version in commit titles and bodies, `## Todo` /
39	   `## Done` entries, and chores headers — all written
40	   while the work is in progress, i.e. before it lands.
41	   But version numbers are subject to change: in a public,
42	   merge-based flow (e.g. Linux), the version a change
43	   ships under is only fixed when it merges into `main`,
44	   so the landing version can't be anticipated while the
45	   work is underway. Pervasive version-in-text is
46	   therefore fragile for any non-linear / multi-contributor
47	   workflow. Promoted from Ideas at 0.65.2-0; slated for
48	   the cycle after 0.65.2.
49	   - Open question: what identifies a cycle's commits if
50	     not a pre-assigned version?

52	       A contributors email address would do it, but also
53	       a UUID (short-version) for a contribution. I could
54	       imagine a UUID generated from the initial email/issue
55	       that and then "version number" schema appended to that.
56	   - Surfaces to update once the identifier is chosen:
57	     cycle-protocol.md (title shape, Numbering), AGENTS.md

61	2. **validate-numbering: rename the pair, check all
62	   sequence-managed notes files generically.** `validate-todo`
63	   / `fix-todo` only operate on the single file passed, so a
64	   renumber slip in `bugs.md`, `todo-backlog.md`, or
65	   `todo.md`'s `## Ideas` section passes unnoticed — too weak
66	   for a pre-commit gate. Prereq for the pre-commit doc
67	   validators (Todo "pre-commit: single rule ...").
68	   - Rename the pair: `validate-todo` → `validate-numbering`,
69	     `fix-todo` → `fix-numbering` — they validate numbered-
70	     sequence integrity, not todos specifically.
71	   - Generic detection: for every `#…#` section, validate the
72	     column-0 `^\d+\.␠` entries form a contiguous 1..N run.
73	     Drops the Todo/Bugs special-casing; auto-covers
74	     `## Ideas` and any new numbered section. Keep the
75	     column-0 anchor so indented sub-lists aren't counted.
76	   - Default scope: a fixed list of sequence-managed notes
77	     files (`todo.md`, `todo-backlog.md`, `bugs.md`) so the
78	     no-arg pre-commit run covers them all. Fixed rather than
79	     a `notes/**.md` walk because prose docs
80	     (`cycle-protocol.md`, design notes) carry ordinary
81	     numbered lists that aren't managed sequences — a walk
82	     would false-positive (markdown renders `1. 1. 1.` as
83	     1-2-3, a legitimate prose pattern).
84	   - Override args follow the `--init-from` convention:
85	     positional files/dirs (a dir → its `*.md`) plus an
86	     `@<file>` manifest, additive — for ad-hoc validation of
87	     a specific file.
88	   - Open: revisit fixed-vs-glob at implementation if the
89	     fixed list proves annoying to maintain.
90	3. **pre-commit: single rule (no docs skip) + doc validators.**
91	   The pre-commit (cargo cycle: fmt/clippy/test/install) only
92	   checks code, so it's "skip-able for purely-docs commits" —
93	   but that exception is exactly where checks slip (skipped on
94	   0.62.0-7/-8 until caught). And `vc-x1 push`'s `preflight`
95	   stage re-runs the same cycle, which invites treating push as
96	   the gate rather than a redundant safety-net.
97	   - Adopt one rule, no exception: the pre-commit runs before
98	     Work review on every commit; push's `preflight` is a
99	     safety-net, not the primary gate. (docs: AGENTS.md Cycle
100	     Protocol summary + cycle-protocol.md per-commit-flow.)

106	   - This dissolves the docs exception: with doc validators in

112	     fragile" on fragile version targets.
113	4. **vc-x1 push: validate body opens with an intro paragraph.**
114	   A body whose first line is a bullet (`- file: …`) is a
115	   Prose-Form violation — bodies must open with an intro
116	   paragraph, then bullets. Today such a body trips jj's arg
117	   parser (`jj commit -m "<body>"` reads the leading `-` as a
118	   stray flag) and push fails with an opaque error. Hit on
119	   0.62.0-5.
120	   - Feature, not a parser bug (reframed): push should
121	     *validate* the body opens with a non-dash intro line and
122	     flag its absence with a clear, specific error pointing at
123	     the offending first line — rather than letting jj emit a
124	     confusing one, or quietly accepting a bullet-first body.
125	   - Enforcing the intro is the intended behavior, matching
126	     the Prose-Form convention; we are not "fixing" the parser
127	     to accept bullet-first bodies.
128	   - Workaround until the explicit check lands: prepend a
129	     non-dash intro sentence to the body.
130	5. **vc-x1 push: support new cycle protocol shape (N:1 code↔bot).**
131	   Today push assumes 1:1 symmetric WC commits with shared
132	   title/body. The new cycle protocol has a different shape on
133	   each side:
134	   - code side: fully committed before push (N commits via
135	     merge), nothing to commit at push time
136	   - `.claude` side: one commit with its own message (distinct
137	     from any code commit) and a multi-line `ochid:` listing
138	     all N code commits
```

1	# Bugs
2	
3	Known defects we're aware of but haven't scheduled a fix for.
4	Each entry describes what goes wrong, when, and the cost of
5	the failure. Entries are numbered (`1.` `2.` …) the same way
6	as `## Todo` in `todo.md`; run
7	`vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/bugs.md` to renumber after
8	insert / delete / reorder.
9	
10	## Bugs
11	
12	1. **`finalize` squash silently drops the source journal's
13	   ochids.** `finalize_exec` (`src/finalize.rs`) always runs
14	   `jj squash --ignore-immutable --use-destination-message
15	   --from <source> --into <target>`, so when the source
16	   commit's message carries `ochid:` trailers the squash
17	   discards them with the rest of the message — the
18	   destination's message wins unconditionally.
19	   - **When it bites:** the squash source (`@`) is a
20	     *described* journal instead of a bare trailing-data
21	     snapshot. Observed sequence (fc op log, 2026-06-08):
22	     the bot wrote the journal message via
23	     `jj describe wyrlsuusnyzz` while that change was still
24	     the uncommitted `@`; finalize then squashed
25	     `--from @ --into @-` where `@-` was the previous,
26	     already-pushed journal — destination message won,
27	     journal message + 6 ochids discarded. Finalize's
28	     assumption (`@` is disposable, `@-` holds the real
29	     message) inverts whenever the journal is described on
30	     `@` rather than committed first.
31	   - **Cost:** every code-side commit pointing at the
32	     squashed journal is left with a dangling
33	     `ochid: /.claude/<chid>` — the code↔session cross-link
34	     breaks. Recovery requires op-log surgery on the machine
35	     that still has the original object (this happened in the
36	     fc project: journal `wyrlsuusnyzz`, 6 dangling app
37	     ochids, recovered 2026-06-10 from a backup workspace's
38	     op log plus a `.claude main` force-push).
39	   - **Fix direction:** before squashing, detect `ochid:`
40	     trailers in the source message that the destination
41	     lacks; refuse (or merge the trailers into the
42	     destination message) instead of dropping them.
43	
44	2. **`finalize::surface_previous_failures` is racy and
45	   bounded by "next vc-x1 run".** The current model has
46	   four gaps:
47	   - **Stale forever.** Markers sit on disk until the
48	     next `vc-x1 <anything>` runs. If the user abandons
49	     the workspace (e.g., CI / scheduled use), failures
50	     are never surfaced.
51	   - **Concurrent surface_previous_failures.** Two
52	     `vc-x1` runs racing: both `read_dir`, both print
53	     the marker, one deletes (the other's `remove_file`
54	     silently fails). The user sees the same failure
55	     printed twice.
56	   - **Mid-write torn read.** A `finalize --exec` child
57	     writing a marker while a sibling `vc-x1` is
58	     surfacing could read partial content. Atomic-rename
59	     on write would close this.
60	   - **No notify-at-failure path.** A detached
61	     `finalize --exec` failure only becomes visible when
62	     the user next runs *any* `vc-x1` command — fine for
63	     interactive use, invisible for CI / scheduled use
64	     where there may be no next run.
65	
66	   The exec-child gate in `main.rs` (since 0.52.0-3)
67	   patches one related case — the detached child eating
68	   its own prior markers before the user can see them —
69	   but doesn't address any of the above. Holistic fix
70	   needs locking + atomic writes + maybe a
71	   notify-at-failure path for hands-off use.
72	
73	# References
74	
```

Found building `tests/cli_sync.rs`\n   (worked around there with `git symbolic-ref HEAD\n   refs/heads/main`).\n   - **Fix direction:** init's local bare provisioning sets\n     HEAD to `refs/heads/main` at creation.\n\n4. **`clone` session-remote derivation mismatches init's\n   local naming; relative TARGET breaks the session clone.**\n   Two related defects, both found building\n   `tests/cli_sync.rs`:\n   - `derive_session_url` maps `<x>.git` →\n     `<x>.claude.git`, but `init --repo local` names the\n     session remote `remote-claude.git` — so a dual clone of\n     a locally-init'd project's `remote-code.git` looks for\n     `remote-code.claude.git` and fails.\n   - A relative local-path TARGET fails on the session side\n     regardless: `clone_dual` runs the session `jj git\n     clone` with the just-cloned code repo as cwd, so the\n     relative source no longer resolves. Workaround: pass an\n     absolute TARGET.\n\n# References"
}

103	#[test]
104	fn cli_sync_moves_main_after_peer_push() {
105	    let fx = CliFixture::new("sync-peer-push");
106	    write_jj_config(&fx.home);
107

111	            .current_dir(&fx.base)
112	            .args(["init", "./tr"])
113	            .arg("--repo")
114	            .arg(format!("local={}", fx.base.display())),
115	    );
116	
117	    // Workarounds — see module docs.
118	    set_head_main(&fx.path("remote-code.git"));
119	    set_head_main(&fx.path("remote-claude.git"));

122	        fx.path("remote-code.claude.git"),
123	    )
124	    .expect("symlink session remote");
125	
126	    // vc-x1 clone <code-remote> trB / trA (B first, so its
127	    // main@origin is the pre-push head). Absolute source path —
128	    // the derived session source is resolved from the clone's
129	    // target dir, so a relative path would not resolve.
130	    let code_remote = fx.path("remote-code.git");
131	    for name in ["trB", "trA"] {

134	                .current_dir(&fx.base)
135	                .arg("clone")

143	
144	    // Change + vc-x1 push on trA. `--from review` skips the cargo
145	    // preflight (the fixture is not a Rust project); `--no-finalize`
146	    // stops before finalize-claude (out of scope here, and its
147	    // detached form logs to a fixed /tmp path); PATH carries the
148	    // binary under test for push's internal `vc-x1` calls.
149	    fs::write(tr_a.join("from-a.txt"), "from clone A\n").expect("write trA change");
150	    run_ok(fx.cmd().current_dir(&tr_a).env("PATH", test_path()).args([
151	        "push",
152	        "main",

154	        "review",
155	        "--title",
156	        "feat: from clone A",
157	        "--body",

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/tests/cli_sync.rs",
  "content": "//! CLI subprocess tests for `vc-x1 sync` — the \"two machines\" flow.\n//!\n//! Drives the whole scenario through the binary: `vc-x1 init` a\n//! project with local bare remotes, `vc-x1 clone` it twice (trA /\n//! trB), change + `vc-x1 push` on trA, then `vc-x1 sync` on trB and\n//! verify trB's `main` moved to the pushed head. Raw `jj` is used\n//! only to inspect results, never to mutate.\n//!\n//! Two init/clone incompatibilities are worked around inline (both\n//! recorded in `notes/bugs.md`):\n//!\n//! - init's local bare remotes leave HEAD at `refs/heads/master`\n//!   while the pushed branch is `main`, so a later `jj git clone`\n//!   has no default branch to auto-track and `vc-x1 clone` errors\n//!   → point HEAD at `main` with `git symbolic-ref`.\n//! - init names the session remote `remote-claude.git`, but clone\n//!   derives `<code-source-stem>.claude.git` → symlink\n//!   `remote-code.claude.git` → `remote-claude.git`.\n\nmod common;\n\nuse std::fs;\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse std::process::Command;\n\nuse common::{CliFixture, run_ok};\n\n/// Directory holding the `vc-x1` binary under test, for prepending\n/// to `PATH` so subcommands that re-invoke `vc-x1` (push preflight,\n/// finalize) find the same binary.\nfn bin_dir() -> PathBuf {\n    Path::new(env!(\"CARGO_BIN_EXE_vc-x1\"))\n        .parent()\n        .expect(\"binary has a parent dir\")\n        .to_path_buf()\n}\n\n/// `PATH` value with the test binary's dir prepended.\nfn test_path() -> String {\n    let orig = std::env::var(\"PATH\").unwrap_or_default();\n    format!(\"{}:{orig}\", bin_dir().display())\n}\n\n/// Run `jj <args>` in `dir` (inspection only) with `HOME` pointed\n/// at the fixture's isolated home; assert success and return\n/// trimmed stdout.\nfn jj(home: &Path, dir: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> String {\n    let out = Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args(args)\n        .env(\"HOME\", home)\n        .current_dir(dir)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"spawn jj\");\n    assert!(\n        out.status.success(),\n        \"jj {args:?} failed in {}: {}\",\n        dir.display(),\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)\n    );\n    String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string()\n}\n\n/// Resolve `rev` to its short commit id in `repo`.\nfn cid(home: &Path, repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> String {\n    jj(\n        home,\n        repo,\n        &[\"log\", \"-r\", rev, \"--no-graph\", \"-T\", \"commit_id.short(12)\"],\n    )\n}\n\n/// Write a minimal jj user config under the fixture's isolated HOME\n/// so jj invocations spawned inside `vc-x1` have an identity.\nfn write_jj_config(home: &Path) {\n    let dir = home.join(\".config/jj\");\n    fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect(\"mkdir jj config dir\");\n    fs::write(\n        dir.join(\"config.toml\"),\n        \"[user]\\nname = \\\"cli-test\\\"\\nemail = \\\"cli-test@example.com\\\"\\n\",\n    )\n    .expect(\"write jj config\");\n}\n\n/// Point a bare remote's HEAD at `main` (init leaves it at\n/// `refs/heads/master` — see module docs).\nfn set_head_main(bare: &Path) {\n    let out = Command::new(\"git\")\n        .args([\"symbolic-ref\", \"HEAD\", \"refs/heads/main\"])\n        .current_dir(bare)\n        .output()\n        .expect(\"spawn git symbolic-ref\");\n    assert!(\n        out.status.success(),\n        \"git symbolic-ref failed in {}: {}\",\n        bare.display(),\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)\n    );\n}\n\n/// Fixture state after the shared peer-push setup: two clones of\n/// one init'ed project, with trA's pushed head recorded.\nstruct PeerPush {\n    fx: CliFixture,\n    tr_b: PathBuf,\n    /// Commit id of the change trA pushed to the shared remote.\n    pushed: String,\n}\n\n/// Shared setup: `vc-x1 init` with local bare remotes, `vc-x1\n/// clone` trB then trA, change + `vc-x1 push` on trA.\n///\n/// Push notes: `--from review` skips the cargo preflight (the\n/// fixture is not a Rust project); `--no-finalize` stops before\n/// finalize-claude (out of scope, and its detached form logs to a\n/// fixed /tmp path); PATH carries the binary under test for push's\n/// internal `vc-x1` calls.\nfn setup_peer_push(tag: &str) -> PeerPush {\n    let fx = CliFixture::new(tag);\n    write_jj_config(&fx.home);\n\n    // vc-x1 init ./tr --repo local=<base>\n    run_ok(\n        fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&fx.base)\n            .args([\"init\", \"./tr\"])\n            .arg(\"--repo\")\n            .arg(format!(\"local={}\", fx.base.display())),\n    );\n\n    // Workarounds — see module docs.\n    set_head_main(&fx.path(\"remote-code.git\"));\n    set_head_main(&fx.path(\"remote-claude.git\"));\n    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(\n        fx.path(\"remote-claude.git\"),\n        fx.path(\"remote-code.claude.git\"),\n    )\n    .expect(\"symlink session remote\");\n\n    // vc-x1 clone <code-remote> trB / trA (B first, so its\n    // main@origin is the pre-push head). Absolute source path —\n    // the derived session source is resolved from the clone's\n    // target dir, so a relative path would not resolve.\n    let code_remote = fx.path(\"remote-code.git\");\n    for name in [\"trB\", \"trA\"] {\n        run_ok(\n            fx.cmd()\n                .current_dir(&fx.base)\n                .arg(\"clone\")\n                .arg(&code_remote)\n                .arg(name),\n        );\n    }\n    let tr_a = fx.path(\"trA\");\n    let tr_b = fx.path(\"trB\");\n    let pre_main = cid(&fx.home, &tr_b, \"main\");\n\n    fs::write(tr_a.join(\"from-a.txt\"), \"from clone A\\n\").expect(\"write trA change\");\n    run_ok(fx.cmd().current_dir(&tr_a).env(\"PATH\", test_path()).args([\n        \"push\",\n        \"main\",\n        \"--from\",\n        \"review\",\n        \"--title\",\n        \"feat: from clone A\",\n        \"--body\",\n        \"add from-a.txt\",\n        \"--yes\",\n        \"--no-finalize\",\n    ]));\n    let pushed = cid(&fx.home, &tr_a, \"main\");\n    assert_ne!(pre_main, pushed, \"trA's push should advance the remote\");\n\n    PeerPush { fx, tr_b, pushed }\n}\n\n/// Assert trB ended in the fully-synced state: code-repo `main` at\n/// the pushed head with `@` repositioned onto it, and the session\n/// repo's `@` repositioned onto its `main`.\nfn assert_trb_synced(p: &PeerPush) {\n    let home = &p.fx.home;\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(home, &p.tr_b, \"main\"),\n        p.pushed,\n        \"trB's main should move to trA's pushed head\"\n    );\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(home, &p.tr_b, \"@-\"),\n        p.pushed,\n        \"trB's @ should be repositioned onto the new main\"\n    );\n    let claude = p.tr_b.join(\".claude\");\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(home, &claude, \"@-\"),\n        cid(home, &claude, \"main\"),\n        \"trB/.claude's @ should be repositioned onto its main\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Apply mode (`sync --no-check`): trB converges — passes today.\n#[test]\nfn cli_sync_no_check_moves_main_after_peer_push() {\n    let p = setup_peer_push(\"sync-nocheck-peer-push\");\n    run_ok(\n        p.fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&p.tr_b)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .args([\"sync\", \"--no-check\"]),\n    );\n    assert_trb_synced(&p);\n}\n\n/// Default mode (plain `vc-x1 sync`, the invocation a user\n/// reaches for): trB must end fully synced, exactly as in the\n/// t1A/t1B transcript (2026-07-02) where it did not.\n///\n/// Ignored until the 0.67.0 single-mode sync lands: today the\n/// default is `--check`, whose fetch auto-ffs `main` but skips the\n/// reposition step, leaving `@-` on the pre-fetch tip. Run\n/// explicitly with `cargo test -- --ignored` to see the failure.\n#[test]\n#[ignore = \"fails until 0.67.0-2 single-mode sync (default must converge + reposition)\"]\nfn cli_sync_default_moves_main_and_at_after_peer_push() {\n    let p = setup_peer_push(\"sync-default-peer-push\");\n    run_ok(\n        p.fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&p.tr_b)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .arg(\"sync\"),\n    );\n    assert_trb_synced(&p);\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Found it — it's not an environment variable, it's your cargo config: `~/.cargo/config.toml:7` has `rustc-wrapper = "sccache"`. The `RUSTC_WRAPPER` env var *overrides* that config entry, and setting it to empty disables the wrapper entirely, so cargo calls `rustc` directly.

I reached for it when `cargo test` failed with `sccache: error: Operation not permitted` under the session sandbox — sccache talks to a background server over a socket outside the sandbox's writable paths. One note: a later run with the sandbox disabled *also* hit the sccache error, which suggests the sccache server got into a bad state (the bot thinks it was started/probed under the sandbox and is now wedged). If plain `cargo build` fails for you in a normal terminal, `sccache --stop-server` should clear it; the next cargo invocation restarts it fresh.

For my remaining runs I'll keep using `RUSTC_WRAPPER=` — it's per-invocation, touches nothing on disk, and just means those builds skip the compile cache.

> AGENT

Good — so sccache is fine on your side, and the failure was purely the sandbox blocking its socket. I'll keep the `RUSTC_WRAPPER=` override only for sandboxed runs and stop speculating about the server.

Back to where we stood, the cycle state is:

- **Failing test demonstrated**: `cli_sync_default_moves_main_and_at_after_peer_push` — your exact invocation (plain `vc-x1 sync` on trB after trA pushed) — fails today exactly as in your transcript: `main` moved but `@-` stayed on the pre-fetch tip. It's `#[ignore]`d with reason "fails until 0.67.0-2 single-mode sync", so the suite stays green; `cargo test -- --ignored` shows the red. The passing `--no-check` variant shares the same setup/assert helpers, so the only difference between the two tests is the flag.
- **Preparation (`0.67.0-0`) edits ready for review**: chores `Commits:` backfill + new section header, `## In Progress` block with the ladder, two follow-up Todos, two new bugs.md entries, version bump to `0.67.0-0`.

I'd commit `-0` with just the prep files (`Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `notes/todo.md`, `notes/bugs.md`, `notes/chores/chores-13.md`), leaving both test files in `@` for `-1`. Ready to commit — please review.

> DEVELOPER

What is your definition of a "sandboxed run"?

> AGENT

A "sandboxed run" is any shell command I execute through my Bash tool while the session's sandbox is on — which it has been since you ran `/sandbox` earlier ("Sandbox enabled with auto-allow"). The sandbox is an OS-level confinement wrapped around each command I run, with two kinds of restrictions:

- **Filesystem:** reads are broadly allowed, but *writes* are limited to an allowlist — essentially this project directory, my session scratchpad under `/tmp/claude-1000/...`, and a few housekeeping paths. Anything else looks like a read-only filesystem to the command. That's what produced the two failures you saw: jj trying to create `~/.config/jj/repos/<id>` (its per-repo "secure config") got `Read-only file system`, and sccache got `Operation not permitted` talking to its server socket.
- **Network:** only an allowlisted set of hosts (github.com, crates.io, docs.rs, etc.).

Two important boundaries of the definition:

- It applies to **my tool invocations only**. Your terminal, your `cargo build`, and anything you run yourself are never sandboxed — which is exactly why your `cargo clean; cargo build` worked fine while my identical command failed.
- It's **per-command escapable**: I can run a specific command unsandboxed, which asks your permission (that's how the full test suite passed earlier — the in-process sync tests spawn jj, which needs to write `~/.config/jj/repos`).

So concretely for this project: sandboxed runs are fine for cargo *builds* if I disable the sccache wrapper (`RUSTC_WRAPPER=`), but any test that shells out to jj against a fresh repo needs either an unsandboxed run or a `HOME` override into the fixture (which is what `tests/cli_sync.rs` does — one of the reasons its helpers set `HOME` explicitly).

> DEVELOPER

I did that because I complained to claude-web that I was annoyed that
for every command I had to approve when you used $PWD and that was a
suggested fix. Is there a better way?

> AGENT

Yes — there's a mechanism aimed at exactly this. The prompts you were seeing come from Claude Code's permission system: every Bash command is matched against allow/deny rules in `.claude/settings.json`, and anything unmatched asks you. Compound commands (`cd … && …`, `$PWD` interpolations, pipelines) are especially prompt-prone because they don't match simple prefix rules.

The sandbox-with-auto-allow you enabled is one fix — "anything sandboxed runs without asking" — but as this session showed, it trades prompts for breakage in jj-heavy work (jj wants to write `~/.config/jj/repos`, sccache wants its socket), and then I end up asking you to approve *unsandboxed* escapes instead, so the prompt count doesn't really drop for this project.

The better fit is a **permission allowlist**: add `Bash(jj *)`, `Bash(cargo *)`, etc. rules to this project's `.claude/settings.json` so the commands this workflow actually uses run without prompting, sandbox off. It's reviewable, committed with the repo, and scoped — anything outside the list still asks. There's a built-in helper for it: the `/fewer-permission-prompts` skill scans my past transcripts in this project for the read-only commands I actually run and writes a prioritized allowlist into `.claude/settings.json`.

Two caveats worth knowing:

- Allowlists match commands, so mutating tools you trust here (`jj commit`, `cargo test`, `vc-x1 …`) can be listed too — that's your call on how permissive to be; the skill leans read-only and we can add the rest by hand.
- Keeping the sandbox on *as well* is fine in principle, but for this project I'd turn it off — the test harness spawns jj against fresh repos constantly, and each one trips the write restriction.

Want me to run `/fewer-permission-prompts` and then hand-add the project's workhorse commands (`jj`, `cargo`, `vc-x1`) for you to review?

> DEVELOPER

What should we do aoubt `"autoAllowBashIfSandboxed": true` since we're going to turn it off? But if `$PWD` are going to byte me again I'll leave it?

> AGENT

Leave it. `autoAllowBashIfSandboxed` is inert while `sandbox.enabled` is `false` — it only means "when the sandbox *is* on, don't prompt for sandboxed commands". Keeping it `true` just preserves your preference: if you ever flip the sandbox back on (`/sandbox`), it comes back in the no-prompt mode you liked, with zero effect in the meantime.

On `$PWD` biting you again: those prompts weren't caused by `$PWD` itself — a command like `jj st -R $PWD` is matched as a plain string against the allow patterns, and `Bash(jj *)` covers it fine. What actually falls through the patterns is: compound commands (`cd x && jj …`), env-var prefixes (`RUSTC_WRAPPER= cargo …`), and commands not starting with an allowed token. The new allowlist (`jj`, `cargo`, `vc-x1`, `./target/*/vc-x1`) covers this project's bread and butter, and the project conventions already steer me away from `cd`-compounds. If some shape still prompts repeatedly, we add that one pattern rather than re-enabling the sandbox.

So: `settings.json` (committed) has the five allow rules; `settings.local.json` has the sandbox off with auto-allow preserved for the future. If that looks right, we're back at the `0.67.0-0` Preparation commit, which is still awaiting your review — prep files only (`Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `notes/todo.md`, `notes/bugs.md`, `notes/chores/chores-13.md`), with the test files (including the new ignored failing test) left in `@` for `-1`.

> AGENT

You're right — I said "committed" loosely. Since this project keeps `.claude/` as its own jj repo, `settings.json` is a new tracked file in the *session* repo's working copy, and it'll actually get recorded when the next session commit lands (the finalize at the next `vc-x1 push`). So it ends up versioned and pushed with the journal — which is what we want for the shared allowlist — just via the `.claude` repo rather than the app repo.

One thing your `jj st` reveals that's worth a decision: `settings.local.json` is *also* tracked (shows as `M`). That file is meant to be machine-local, personal state — it's where "Approve always" answers and the sandbox toggle accumulate — and Claude Code's convention elsewhere is to gitignore it. Tracked, it means every stray one-off approval you ever click gets committed and pushed with the session repo. If you'd rather keep it local-only, I can add a `.claude/.gitignore` entry for `settings.local.json` and `jj file untrack` it. Or leave it tracked if you like having that history in the journal — your call; functionally both work.
