I work on different machines and kinda expect the same behavior on all of them, which means I want to commit it, but that's how I want to work others might have different opinions. And I see that it's huge, not sure what to do, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good instinct on both counts. The size problem and the sync-across-machines goal actually point to the same cleanup: the file is huge because it's a *sediment layer* — every time you clicked "approve always", Claude Code appended that exact command as a rule. Look at what's in there: `Bash(sed -n '1483,1486p' src/push.rs)`, `Bash(echo "exit=$?")`… rules that will never match anything again. Maybe 10 of the 90 entries carry durable intent, and the new wildcards in `settings.json` subsume most of those.

My suggestion — use the two files by *role*, not by accident of history:

- **`settings.json` = the durable, deliberate policy** (tracked in the `.claude` repo, so every machine that clones the project gets it). Migrate the keepers from local into it: `WebSearch`, the `WebFetch(domain:…)` entries (github.com, docs.jj-vcs.dev, crates.io, docs.rs, raw.githubusercontent.com, docs.kernel.org, claude.ai), `Read(//tmp/**)`, and the already-added `jj`/`cargo`/`vc-x1` wildcards. Maybe `Bash(awk *)` and `Bash(bash notes/substep-test.sh)` if you want them.
- **`settings.local.json` = disposable scratch** where future one-off approvals land. Prune it now to almost nothing (the sandbox block can go entirely — off is the default, and `/sandbox` recreates it on demand). Accept that it regrows, and sweep it into `settings.json` or delete entries occasionally — same spirit as your `## Done` → `done.md` migration beat.

Tracking the local file is then harmless — it stays small between sweeps, and committing it gives you the same behavior everywhere, which is what you want. (The name "local" stops being accurate for your workflow, but the mechanics don't care: both files' `permissions.allow` lists are unioned.)

Want me to do that migration and prune now?

> DEVELOPER

Based on what you say it should be added to .gitignore, but in reality this dual repo setup is my "invention", the intention of Anthropic is that these are both "local" and saved in ~/.claude/projects/, correct?

> AGENT

Close, but the standard design is a bit different — it splits by *purpose*, and your invention changes where one half lives:

- **`~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-path>/`** — this is what Anthropic intends as machine-local: session *data* (transcripts like those `.jsonl` files, session state). Never versioned, never shared.
- **`<project>/.claude/settings.json`** — intended to live in the project and be **checked into the project's own repo**, shared with collaborators. In a stock setup it'd be a normal tracked file of the app repo.
- **`<project>/.claude/settings.local.json`** — intended personal-per-project; stock Claude Code even auto-adds it to the app repo's `.gitignore` for you.
- **`~/.claude/settings.json`** — user-global, machine-local.

So Anthropic's intent isn't "both settings files are local under `~/.claude/projects/`" — settings were always meant to sit in the project, with the `.json`/`.local.json` split being shared-vs-personal. What your dual-repo invention changes is that `<project>/.claude/` is itself a repo (with `~/.claude/projects/<path>` symlinked into it), so *everything* in it — session journal **and** both settings files — gets versioned and pushed in the session repo instead of settings riding in the app repo.

That makes your earlier call coherent: in your workflow the "personal machine-local" category barely exists — you *want* one behavior across machines, and the `.claude` repo is precisely your mechanism for syncing per-project bot state. So ignoring `settings.local.json` would follow stock convention but fight your design. Tracking both, with the roles we discussed (`settings.json` = curated policy, `settings.local.json` = approval sediment, swept periodically), is the consistent choice for this setup — someone with the stock one-repo layout would instead gitignore the local file, which is the "others might have different opinions" case you anticipated.

Shall I do the migration/prune of the two files now, and then we get back to the pending `0.67.0-0` commit?

> DEVELOPER

We'll do the pruning but I wonder what the goal should be. My thought is that saving the conversation with the code allows a much better historical record and allows the possibility to answer, why did the user do xyz. That is the whole idea and intention of this dual repo mode. And vc-x1 is the tool that might someday allow you to explore the connection made possible by the ochid's. Thoughts?

> AGENT

I think the idea is genuinely sound, and this very session is a good exhibit for it. Consider what the app repo will record of 0.67.0: a chores section saying sync became single-mode, stop-on-error, with a revert command. What it *can't* record is how that decision actually formed: your t1A/t1B transcript, my two wrong reproductions (I tested `--no-check` because I "knew how to use the program"), the realization that check-mode's read-only contract was already a fiction, your rejection of verify-then-apply as inherently racy, then of auto-revert as evidence-destroying. That derivation — including the dead ends — is the real "why did the user do xyz", and it exists only in the conversation. Commit messages are the compressed verdict; the journal is the trial record.

The ochid is what makes this more than an archive. A code commit carrying `ochid: /.claude/<chid>` is a durable, rebase-surviving pointer from *change* to *deliberation* — every clone can resolve it. What's missing today is exactly the tool you're gesturing at: the link lands you on a journal commit, which is a large `.jsonl` blob; nobody will read that raw. The future vc-x1 capability is the dereference: given a code commit, resolve its ochid, extract the conversation slice that produced it (the transcripts are timestamped, so a commit can be mapped to a window of turns), maybe summarize or index it. Then "why?" becomes a query instead of an act of archaeology. Notably, bug #1 in bugs.md — the finalize squash dropping ochids — is exactly the failure mode that severs this chain, which is why guarding those trailers (0.65.2) mattered more than it looked.

Two honest caveats. First, the record is raw and enormous — its value depends on that future dereference tool existing; until then you're banking evidence at low cost, which jj/git handles fine. Second, publishing deliberation is a real choice: transcripts contain everything (paths, mistakes, half-formed ideas), and a public `.claude` repo shares it all. You've clearly accepted that; a collaborator might not — same "others might have different opinions" boundary as the settings question.

And that gives the pruning question its principle: **the journal is the record of deliberation; settings are policy.** Transcripts belong in history verbatim — never prune those. `settings.local.json`'s approval sediment isn't deliberation, it's UI residue; sweeping it into a curated `settings.json` loses no "why" (and the sweep itself gets recorded in the journal anyway). So the goal for pruning: keep the record complete, keep the policy legible.

Shall I do the sweep now — migrate the keepers into `settings.json`, empty the sediment out of `settings.local.json`, drop the sandbox block — and then we return to the pending `0.67.0-0` commit?

> DEVELOPER

Yes, do the sweep and then let's get back to the commit

> AGENT

Good catch — that's exactly the right suspect, and it's a real defect in the 0.66.0-vintage test, not flakiness. Diagnosis:

`sync_code_skips_rebase_without_flag` drives `sync_repos` **in-process** with a non-empty `@` and no `--rebase`, which lands in `confirm_rebase` (src/sync.rs:542). That function's guard is "prompt only if stdin is a terminal" — and when *you* run `cargo test` in a real terminal, the test harness inherits your TTY stdin, so it blocks on `read_line`. Your enter key answers "" → "no" → the skip path continues and the test passes. It never hung for me only because my shell tool's stdin isn't a TTY, so `is_terminal()` returned false and the prompt was skipped — an environment-dependent test.

The minimal deterministic fix: make `confirm_rebase` treat the test harness like a non-interactive session:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01KiNF2D1K78V25kW4JiqVB9
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/sync.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Ask whether to rebase a non-empty `@`, but only on a TTY.\n///\n/// Returns `Ok(false)` without prompting when stdin isn't a terminal\n/// (scripts): the caller then skips + informs rather than blocking on\n/// `read_line`. A `y`/`yes` (case-insensitive) answer confirms.\nfn confirm_rebase(repo: &Path) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    if !std::io::stdin().is_terminal() {\n        return Ok(false);\n    }",
  "new_string": "/// Ask whether to rebase a non-empty `@`, but only on a TTY.\n///\n/// Returns `Ok(false)` without prompting when stdin isn't a terminal\n/// (scripts): the caller then skips + informs rather than blocking on\n/// `read_line`. A `y`/`yes` (case-insensitive) answer confirms.\n///\n/// Under `cargo test` the harness inherits the invoking terminal's\n/// stdin, so an in-process test reaching this path would block on\n/// `read_line` waiting for the user — the `cfg!(test)` arm pins the\n/// non-interactive answer instead.\nfn confirm_rebase(repo: &Path) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    if cfg!(test) || !std::io::stdin().is_terminal() {\n        return Ok(false);\n    }"
}
```

> AGENT

`-j` doesn't do what it looks like there: it controls **cargo's build parallelism** — how many compilation jobs run concurrently — not test execution. Once a test binary is built, cargo hands off to Rust's libtest harness, which schedules tests on its own thread pool (default = number of CPUs). To serialize the *tests* you pass flags through to the harness, after the `--` separator:

```
cargo test -- --test-threads=1
```

(or `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1 cargo test`). You can combine them: `cargo test -j 1 -- --test-threads=1` would build serially *and* run tests serially — two independent knobs for two different phases.

And yes — the un-ignored test failing with `@-` left on the old tip is exactly the red we want until `0.67.0-2` lands. One bookkeeping consequence: you edited `tests/cli_sync.rs` to remove the `#[ignore]`, and that file is part of the pending `-1` commit, so it needs to go back before we commit (the suite must be green at `-1`). Let me check the working copy:

> AGENT

Two separate libtest behaviors explain that:

1. **A failing test doesn't stop the run.** libtest always runs every scheduled test and reports the tally at the end — there's no fail-fast by default (serial vs parallel doesn't change that; `--test-threads=1` only made them take turns, hence slower). A stable fail-fast option doesn't exist yet; people who want it usually just rerun the one test by name.
2. **The ignored test ran at all** because your invocation must have included `--include-ignored` (that's also why the summary says `0 ignored`). `#[ignore]` means "skip by default", not "never run": plain `cargo test` skips it and reports `1 ignored`, `cargo test -- --ignored` runs *only* the ignored ones, `--include-ignored` runs everything. That's the mechanism the plan leans on — the red test stays in the tree, visible on demand, and flips to always-run when `-2` removes the attribute.

So everything you're seeing is expected: green suite by default, deliberate red reachable explicitly. The `-1` commit description is in my previous message awaiting your OK.

3	use super::*;

6	/// Default flags: neither `--check` nor `--no-check` set (the
7	/// implicit default is check mode), bookmark "main", remote "origin",
8	/// no `-R` and no `--scope` (caller will resolve via the
9	/// workspace-default scope), `--quiet` off.
10	#[test]
11	fn parse_defaults() {
12	    use clap::Parser;
13	    #[derive(Parser)]
14	    struct Cli {
15	        #[command(flatten)]
16	        args: SyncArgs,
17	    }
18	    let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test"]).unwrap();
19	    assert!(!cli.args.check);
20	    assert!(!cli.args.no_check);
21	    assert!(!cli.args.quiet);
22	    assert!(!cli.args.rebase);
23	    assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, "main");
24	    assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, "origin");
25	    assert!(cli.args.scope.is_none());
26	}
27

38	    assert!(cli.args.rebase);

  "new_string": "//! The `sync` subcommand: fetch + classify + rebase / fast-forward\n//! a workspace's repos against their remotes, then reposition `@`\n//! onto the synced bookmark.\n//!\n//! Sync is a single atomic operation — verify-then-act happens\n//! inside one invocation against one fetch snapshot (a separate\n//! check-then-apply pair of runs would race the remote). The\n//! hidden deprecated `--check` flag preserves the old verify-only\n//! mode for `push`'s preflight until that is rewired in-process.\n//! On any failure every repo is reverted to its pre-sync op via\n//! `jj op restore` (stop-on-error + `vc-x1 revert` replaces this\n//! in 0.67.0-3)."
}

  "old_string": "    // Phase 3 — act (subprocess output streams through as usual).\n    // `act_on_state` short-circuits when `params.no_check` is false,\n    // so check mode is a true no-op here. Repositioning `@` onto the\n    // synced bookmark happens after `run_plan` returns (see\n    // `sync_repos`), outside the revert region.\n    for ctx in &ctxs {\n        act_on_state(ctx, params)?;\n    }\n\n    // Phase 4 — check mode is fatal when action would be needed.\n    // Apply mode (`--no-check`) ran the action above and is done.\n    if !params.no_check && any_action_needed {\n        let n_action = ctxs\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }))\n            .count();\n        let noun = if n_action == 1 { \"repo\" } else { \"repos\" };\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"sync: {n_action} {noun} need action (see above) — \\\n             resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run\"\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())",
  "new_string": "    // Phase 3 — act (subprocess output streams through as usual).\n    // `act_on_state` short-circuits in deprecated verify-only mode,\n    // making it a true no-op here. Repositioning `@` onto the synced\n    // bookmark happens after `run_plan` returns (see `sync_repos`),\n    // outside the revert region.\n    for ctx in &ctxs {\n        act_on_state(ctx, params)?;\n    }\n\n    // Phase 4 — verify-only mode is fatal when action would be\n    // needed. The normal sync ran the action above and is done.\n    if params.check && any_action_needed {\n        let n_action = ctxs\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }))\n            .count();\n        let noun = if n_action == 1 { \"repo\" } else { \"repos\" };\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"sync: {n_action} {noun} need action (see above) — \\\n             resolve with `vc-x1 sync` and re-run\"\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())"
}

574	///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the
575	///   pre-fetch state.
576	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, params: &SyncParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
577	    let repo = &ctx.path;
578	    let remote_rev = format!("{}@{}", params.bookmark, params.remote);
579	    match &ctx.state {
580	        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),
581	        State::Behind { .. } => {
582	            if params.no_check {
583	                info!("{}: fast-forwarding '{}'", repo.display(), params.bookmark);
584	                run(
585	                    "jj",
586	                    &[
587	                        "bookmark",
588	                        "set",
589	                        &params.bookmark,
590	                        "-r",
591	                        &remote_rev,
592	                        "-R",
593	                        &repo_str(repo),
594	                    ],
595	                    Path::new("."),
596	                )?;
597	            }
598	            Ok(())
599	        }
600	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
601	            if params.no_check {
602	                // `local` is either a single commit id or a comma-joined list
603	                // of heads when the bookmark is conflicted. Pick the head
604	                // that isn't the remote — that's the local-only tip. The
605	                // comma-joined path covers the jj post-fetch divergence
606	                // shape (local bookmark conflicted between old local head
607	                // and freshly-fetched remote head).
608	                let local_head = local
609	                    .split(',')
610	                    .find(|h| *h != remote)
611	                    .unwrap_or(local.as_str());
612	                info!(
613	                    "{}: rebasing {local_head} onto {remote_rev}",
614	                    repo.display()
615	                );
616	                run(
617	                    "jj",
618	                    &[
619	                        "rebase",
620	                        "-b",
621	                        local_head,
622	                        "-d",
623	                        &remote_rev,
624	                        "-R",
625	                        &repo_str(repo),
626	                    ],
627	                    Path::new("."),
628	                )?;
629	                if has_conflicts(repo)? {
630	                    return Err(format!("{}: rebase produced conflicts", repo.display()).into());
631	                }
632	            }
633	            Ok(())
634	        }
635	    }
636	}

410	/// Reposition `@` onto the freshly-synced bookmark after a successful
411	/// apply-mode sync.
412	///
413	/// Dispatches on repo role — the caller has already gated on apply
414	/// mode:
415	///
416	/// - **session (bot) sub-repo** → always `jj new main`
417	///   (see `reposition_session`).
418	/// - **any other repo** → move `@` onto the synced `bookmark` under
419	///   the code-repo safety rules (see `reposition_code`).
420	fn reposition_at(
421	    repo: &Path,
422	    bookmark: &str,
423	    params: &SyncParams,
424	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
425	    if is_session_repo(repo) {
426	        reposition_session(repo)
427	    } else {
428	        reposition_code(repo, bookmark, params.rebase)
429	    }
430	}

  "new_string": "/// Overrides: `--bookmark`, `--remote` honored.\n#[test]\nfn parse_overrides() {\n    use clap::Parser;\n    #[derive(Parser)]\n    struct Cli {\n        #[command(flatten)]\n        args: SyncArgs,\n    }\n    let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\", \"--bookmark\", \"dev\", \"--remote\", \"upstream\"]).unwrap();\n    assert!(!cli.args.check);\n    assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, \"dev\");\n    assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, \"upstream\");\n    assert!(cli.args.scope.is_none());\n}\n\n/// Hidden deprecated `--check` still parses (push preflight relies\n/// on it until rewired in-process) and flows through to params.\n#[test]\nfn parse_check_flag() {\n    use clap::Parser;\n    #[derive(Parser)]\n    struct Cli {\n        #[command(flatten)]\n        args: SyncArgs,\n    }\n    let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\", \"--check\"]).unwrap();\n    assert!(cli.args.check);\n    assert!(SyncParams::from(&cli.args).check);\n}\n\n/// `--no-check` is gone — a stale script invocation must fail\n/// loudly rather than silently flip semantics.\n#[test]\nfn parse_no_check_rejected() {\n    use clap::Parser;\n    #[derive(Parser)]\n    struct Cli {\n        #[command(flatten)]\n        args: SyncArgs,\n    }\n    assert!(Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\", \"--no-check\"]).is_err());\n}"

Run\n/// explicitly with `cargo test -- --ignored` to see the failure.\n#[test]\n#[ignore = \"fails until 0.67.0-2 single-mode sync (default must converge + reposition)\"]\nfn cli_sync_default_moves_main_and_at_after_peer_push() {\n    let p = setup_peer_push(\"sync-default-peer-push\");\n    run_ok(\n        p.fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&p.tr_b)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .arg(\"sync\"),\n    );\n    assert_trb_synced(&p);\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Plain `vc-x1 sync` (the invocation a user reaches for): trB\n/// must end fully synced — main at trA's pushed head, `@`\n/// repositioned in both repos. Encodes the t1A/t1B transcript\n/// (2026-07-02) where the pre-0.67.0 check-mode default left `@-`\n/// on the pre-fetch tip; red until 0.67.0-2 made sync single-mode.\n#[test]\nfn cli_sync_default_moves_main_and_at_after_peer_push() {\n    let p = setup_peer_push(\"sync-default-peer-push\");\n    run_ok(\n        p.fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&p.tr_b)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .arg(\"sync\"),\n    );\n    assert_trb_synced(&p);\n}\n\n/// The hidden deprecated `--check` alias (push preflight's\n/// verify-only shell-out) still parses and still skips the\n/// reposition step: after it runs, trB's `main` has moved (jj's\n/// fetch auto-ffs the tracked bookmark — the mode was never fully\n/// read-only) but `@-` stays on the pre-fetch tip.\n#[test]\nfn cli_sync_check_alias_verifies_only() {\n    let p = setup_peer_push(\"sync-check-peer-push\");\n    let pre_at_parent = cid(&p.fx.home, &p.tr_b, \"@-\");\n    run_ok(\n        p.fx.cmd()\n            .current_dir(&p.tr_b)\n            .env(\"PATH\", test_path())\n            .args([\"sync\", \"--check\"]),\n    );\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(&p.fx.home, &p.tr_b, \"main\"),\n        p.pushed,\n        \"fetch auto-ff still moves trB's main under --check\"\n    );\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(&p.fx.home, &p.tr_b, \"@-\"),\n        pre_at_parent,\n        \"--check must not reposition @\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// `--no-check` is gone — a stale script invocation must fail\n/// loudly rather than silently flip semantics.\n#[test]\nfn cli_sync_no_check_rejected() {\n    let fx = CliFixture::new(\"sync-no-check-rejected\");\n    run_err(fx.cmd().current_dir(&fx.base).args([\"sync\", \"--no-check\"]));\n}"

182	    /// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes
183	    #[command(long_about = "Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n\n\
184	        Repo set is resolved (in order):\n  \
185	          - `-R` / `--repo`     exact list (back-compat / arbitrary multi-repo)\n  \
186	          - `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` dual-repo roles via `.vc-config.toml`\n  \
187	          - neither             default: `code,bot` when dual, else `code`\n\n\
188	        Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs\n\
189	        action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).\n\
190	        Per repo:\n  \
191	          - up-to-date        nothing to do\n  \
192	          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\n  \
193	          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\n  \
194	          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\n  \
195	          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\n\n\
196	        Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check\n\
197	        explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the\n\
198	        contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.\n\n\
199	        After a successful --no-check sync, `@` is repositioned onto\n\
200	        the synced bookmark: the code repo `jj new`s a clean `@` (or\n\
201	        rebases a dirty one with --rebase / a prompt), the `.claude`\n\
202	        session repo always `jj new main`s. This pass runs outside the\n\
203	        revert region below, so a reposition problem doesn't undo the\n\
204	        successful fetch / fast-forward.\n\n\
205	        On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via\n\
206	        `jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the\n\
207	        revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.\n\n\
208	        Output shape:\n  \
209	          - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)\n  \
210	          - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error\n  \
211	          - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions\n  \
212	          - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success")]
213	    Sync(sync::SyncArgs),

> AGENT

The bug is fixed: plain `vc-x1 sync` now converges and repositions, and the regression test that encoded your t1A/t1B transcript is green and un-ignored. Work summary for review:

- **`src/sync.rs`** — the mode split is gone:
  - `--no-check` removed; the default *is* the atomic sync (fetch → fast-forward/rebase → reposition `@`).
  - `--check` kept as a **hidden** deprecated flag (verify-only) solely for push's preflight shell-out, with docs noting it was never fully read-only (fetch auto-ffs) and pointing at the rewire follow-up.
  - Module/struct/function docs rewritten around the single-operation contract; check-mode's error now says "resolve with `vc-x1 sync`"; `log_state` says "fast-forward/rebase needed" instead of "would …".
  - Auto-revert on failure is untouched here — that's `-3`'s stop-on-error work.
- **`src/sync/tests.rs`** — `parse_overrides`/`parse_defaults` updated; `parse_check_flag` now pins the hidden alias; new `parse_no_check_rejected` (a stale `--no-check` script must fail loudly, not silently change meaning).
- **`src/sync/integration_tests.rs`** — `apply_params()` → `default_params()` (12 call sites); doc tweaks.
- **`tests/cli_sync.rs`** — the default-mode test is un-ignored (primary regression test); the old `--no-check` test replaced by two: `cli_sync_check_alias_verifies_only` (pins the alias's semantics: main auto-ffs, `@` *not* repositioned) and `cli_sync_no_check_rejected`.
- **`src/main.rs`** — sync's `long_about` rewritten (no modes).
- **`src/push.rs`** — preflight doc comment updated (deprecated alias, follow-up pointer); behavior unchanged, and its `vc-x1 sync --check` shell-out still works.
- **`notes/todo.md`** markers, **`Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock`** → `0.67.0-2`.

fmt/clippy/test/install all clean. You may also want to hand-verify on your t1B clone: build is installed, so `vc-x1 sync` there should now land `@` on main in both repos. Ready to commit — please review.

> DEVELOPER

That touched a lot of files!

I verified it works in t1B.d, and why not remove --check if it's hidden?

> AGENT

Both fair points.

**On the file count:** the semantic change is really one file (`src/sync.rs`); the rest is fallout radius — every place that *spelled* the old modes: unit tests parsing the flags, integration-test param helpers, the CLI tests, `main.rs` help text, one push doc comment. That's the cost of renaming part of a CLI contract, and it's why the commit body's file list makes it look bigger than it is: mostly docs and test renames, no behavior outside sync.

**On removing `--check` outright:** because `push` would break *today*. Its preflight stage literally shells out to the installed binary — `run("vc-x1", ["sync", "--check"], root)` at `src/push.rs:899` — so if `-2` deletes the flag, the next `vc-x1 push` (including the one that publishes this very cycle!) dies in preflight with a clap error. Making push not need it isn't a one-liner: the preflight has to become an in-process call, and there's a real design question hiding in it — should push's first stage *be* a full sync (which moves your working copy mid-push), or a classify-only refusal? That's why I parked it as Todo #3 ("push preflight in-process; drop `--check`; revisit push auto-rollback") rather than folding it into `-2`. Hidden-but-alive is the standard deprecation half-step: no new users can discover it, the one in-tree caller keeps working, and the follow-up cycle deletes both ends together.

If you'd rather not carry the alias at all, the alternative is to expand this cycle: give sync a classify-only internal function, have push call it in-process, and delete `--check` — but that drags push redesign questions into what's currently a clean, verified sync change. My recommendation: ship `-2` as reviewed, keep Todo #3 as the very next cycle after 0.67.0 closes.

Shall I write up the commit description for `-2`?

250	
251	/// Default directory (relative to app-repo root) for the push state
252	/// file when `.vc-config.toml` has no `[push]` override.
253	const DEFAULT_STATE_DIR: &str = ".vc-x1";
254	
255	/// Default filename for the push state file under `state_dir`.
256	const DEFAULT_STATE_FILE: &str = "push-state.toml";
257	
258	/// Resolved state-file layout — just the full path today, structured
259	/// so future additions (e.g. a `dir` exposed for `.gitignore`
260	/// coherence checks in 0.37.0-3) have a place to land without
261	/// changing callers.
262	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
263	pub struct StateLayout {
264	    /// Full path `<repo>/<state-dir>/<state-file>`.
265	    pub path: PathBuf,
266	}
267	
268	/// Read `state_dir` / `state_file` from `<repo_root>/.vc-config.toml`
269	/// under the `[push]` section, falling back to defaults (`.vc-x1`
270	/// and `push-state.toml`).
271	///
272	/// Missing config file is not an error — every workspace has the
273	/// defaults applied silently so `vc-x1 push --status` works on a
274	/// fresh clone without ceremony.
275	pub fn resolve_state_layout(repo_root: &Path) -> StateLayout {
276	    let config_path = repo_root.join(".vc-config.toml");
277	    let (dir, file) = if config_path.exists() {
278	        match toml_load(&config_path) {
279	            Ok(map) => {
280	                let dir = map
281	                    .get("push.state-dir")
282	                    .cloned()
283	                    .unwrap_or_else(|| DEFAULT_STATE_DIR.to_string());
284	                let file = map
285	                    .get("push.state-file")
286	                    .cloned()
287	                    .unwrap_or_else(|| DEFAULT_STATE_FILE.to_string());
288	                (dir, file)
289	            }
290	            Err(e) => {
291	                debug!("push: ignoring unparseable .vc-config.toml: {e}");
292	                (
293	                    DEFAULT_STATE_DIR.to_string(),
294	                    DEFAULT_STATE_FILE.to_string(),
295	                )
296	            }
297	        }
298	    } else {
299	        (
300	            DEFAULT_STATE_DIR.to_string(),
301	            DEFAULT_STATE_FILE.to_string(),
302	        )
303	    };
304	    let path = repo_root.join(&dir).join(&file);
305	    StateLayout { path }
306	}
307	
308	/// Persistent state for an in-progress `push` run.
309	///
310	/// Serialized as flat TOML (`key = "value"`) under the dir/file
311	/// configured in `.vc-config.toml`'s `[push]` section. Fields added
312	/// across dev steps are all `Option<_>` so older states remain
313	/// loadable — only `version` / `stage` / `bookmark` / `started_at`
314	/// are required. See the module docstring for which dev step
315	/// introduced which field.
316	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
317	pub struct PushState {
318	    /// State-file format version; must match `STATE_FORMAT_VERSION`
319	    /// to be considered readable.
320	    pub version: u32,
321	    /// The next stage to execute on resume.
322	    pub stage: Stage,
323	    /// Bookmark being advanced by this run (persisted so resume
324	    /// doesn't need `--bookmark` again).
325	    pub bookmark: String,
326	    /// ISO-8601 UTC timestamp captured when the state was first
327	    /// written. Informational — helps the user spot stale state.
328	    pub started_at: String,
329	    /// App-repo changeID captured at `message` stage (before
330	    /// `commit-app` runs). Stable across `jj commit` — becomes the
331	    /// chid of the just-committed change. Used when composing the
332	    /// `.claude` commit's ochid trailer. Added in 0.37.0-2.
333	    pub app_chid: Option<String>,
334	    /// `.claude` repo changeID used by the app-repo commit's ochid
335	    /// trailer. Either the pre-commit `@` chid (when `.claude` has
336	    /// pending changes — becomes `@-` after commit) or the current
337	    /// `@-` chid (when `.claude` is clean — stays stable). Added in
338	    /// 0.37.0-2.
339	    pub claude_chid: Option<String>,
340	    /// Whether `.claude`'s working copy had changes at `message`
341	    /// time. Decides whether `commit-claude` actually runs or
342	    /// skips. Added in 0.37.0-2.
343	    pub claude_had_changes: Option<bool>,
344	    /// `jj op` id of the app repo captured before `commit-app`. On
345	    /// failure in stages 4-6, `jj op restore` rewinds here. Added
346	    /// in 0.37.0-2.
347	    pub op_app: Option<String>,
348	    /// `jj op` id of `.claude` captured before `commit-app`. Same
349	    /// rollback target as `op_app`. Added in 0.37.0-2.
350	    pub op_claude: Option<String>,
351	    /// Composed commit title — persisted so resume doesn't need
352	    /// `--title` re-passed. Set during `message` stage from either
353	    /// `--title` or `$EDITOR`. Added in 0.37.0-4.
354	    pub title: Option<String>,
355	    /// Composed commit body (sans ochid trailer, which each commit
356	    /// stage appends). Persisted alongside title. Multi-line
357	    /// content is escaped for the flat-TOML save format (see
358	    /// `escape_multiline` / `unescape_multiline`). Added in
359	    /// 0.37.0-4.
360	    pub body: Option<String>,
361	}
362	
363	impl PushState {
364	    /// Build a fresh state for a new run.
365	    pub fn new_for(bookmark: &str) -> Self {
366	        PushState {
367	            version: STATE_FORMAT_VERSION,
368	            stage: Stage::first(),
369	            bookmark: bookmark.to_string(),
370	            started_at: Utc::now().to_rfc3339(),
371	            app_chid: None,
372	            claude_chid: None,
373	            claude_had_changes: None,
374	            op_app: None,
375	            op_claude: None,
376	            title: None,
377	            body: None,
378	        }
379	    }
380	
381	    /// Write the state to `path`, creating parent dirs as needed.
382	    ///
383	    /// Values are single-line and contain no `"` characters under
384	    /// normal operation (stage names are kebab-case, chids are
385	    /// ASCII, bookmark names don't contain quotes, timestamps are
386	    /// ASCII). A defensive escape pass replaces any stray `"` with
387	    /// `\"` so the file always parses with `toml_simple`. Optional
388	    /// fields are only emitted when set so older state files don't
389	    /// carry a wall of blank keys.
390	    pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
391	        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
392	            fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
393	        }
394	        let mut content = String::new();
395	        content.push_str("# vc-x1 push state — managed file, do not edit by hand\n");
396	        content.push_str("[push-state]\n");
397	        content.push_str(&format!("version = {}\n", self.version));
398	        content.push_str(&format!("stage = \"{}\"\n", self.stage.as_str()));
399	        content.push_str(&format!("bookmark = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(&self.bookmark)));
400	        content.push_str(&format!(
401	            "started_at = \"{}\"\n",
402	            escape_toml(&self.started_at)
403	        ));
404	        if let Some(v) = &self.app_chid {
405	            content.push_str(&format!("app_chid = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
406	        }
407	        if let Some(v) = &self.claude_chid {
408	            content.push_str(&format!("claude_chid = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
409	        }
410	        if let Some(v) = self.claude_had_changes {
411	            content.push_str(&format!("claude_had_changes = {v}\n"));
412	        }
413	        if let Some(v) = &self.op_app {
414	            content.push_str(&format!("op_app = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
415	        }
416	        if let Some(v) = &self.op_claude {
417	            content.push_str(&format!("op_claude = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
418	        }
419	        if let Some(v) = &self.title {
420	            content.push_str(&format!("title = \"{}\"\n", escape_multiline(v)));
421	        }
422	        if let Some(v) = &self.body {
423	            content.push_str(&format!("body = \"{}\"\n", escape_multiline(v)));
424	        }
425	        fs::write(path, content)?;
426	        debug!("push: wrote state to {}", path.display());
427	        Ok(())
428	    }
429

432	    /// unusable (stale format, missing required keys, unknown stage).
433	    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Option<Self>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
434	        if !path.exists() {
435	            return Ok(None);
436	        }

  "content": "//! Persisted pre-sync snapshot: `<repo>/.vc-x1/sync-state.toml`.\n//!\n//! Sync stops on error instead of auto-reverting, so the pre-sync\n//! `jj op` id must outlive the failed process for `vc-x1 revert`\n//! to consume. Each synced repo carries its own snapshot file —\n//! sync accepts arbitrary repo lists (`-R`, scope), so there is no\n//! single home for a combined file:\n//!\n//! - written for every repo right after the op-id snapshot, before\n//!   any fetch;\n//! - removed on full success (a stale file must not become a\n//!   revert target long after the state moved on);\n//! - left in place on failure — the error report points at it.\n//!\n//! The `.vc-x1/` dir is already gitignored (init writes the\n//! `/.vc-x1` line; push keeps its `push-state.toml` there too).\n\nuse std::fs;\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n\nuse chrono::Utc;\nuse log::debug;\n\nuse crate::toml_simple::toml_load;\n\n/// State-file format version; bump on incompatible change.\nconst STATE_FORMAT_VERSION: u32 = 1;\n\n/// Relative path of the sync state file under a repo root.\nconst STATE_REL_PATH: &str = \".vc-x1/sync-state.toml\";\n\n/// One repo's pre-sync snapshot, as persisted.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct SyncState {\n    /// State-file format version; must match `STATE_FORMAT_VERSION`\n    /// to be considered readable.\n    pub version: u32,\n    /// `jj op` id of the repo captured before the sync acted.\n    pub op_id: String,\n    /// Bookmark the sync was converging.\n    pub bookmark: String,\n    /// Remote the sync was fetching from.\n    pub remote: String,\n    /// ISO-8601 UTC timestamp when the snapshot was taken.\n    /// Informational — helps the user spot stale state.\n    pub started_at: String,\n}\n\n/// Full path of the sync state file for `repo`.\npub fn state_path(repo: &Path) -> PathBuf {\n    repo.join(STATE_REL_PATH)\n}\n\n/// Persist a fresh snapshot for `repo`.\n///\n/// Creates `.vc-x1/` as needed and overwrites any previous file —\n/// a new sync supersedes an older snapshot.\npub fn save(\n    repo: &Path,\n    op_id: &str,\n    bookmark: &str,\n    remote: &str,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let path = state_path(repo);\n    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {\n        fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;\n    }\n    let content = format!(\n        \"# vc-x1 sync state — managed file, do not edit by hand\\n\\\n         [sync-state]\\n\\\n         version = {STATE_FORMAT_VERSION}\\n\\\n         op_id = \\\"{op_id}\\\"\\n\\\n         bookmark = \\\"{bookmark}\\\"\\n\\\n         remote = \\\"{remote}\\\"\\n\\\n         started_at = \\\"{}\\\"\\n\",\n        Utc::now().to_rfc3339()\n    );\n    fs::write(&path, content)?;\n    debug!(\"sync: wrote state to {}\", path.display());\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Load `repo`'s snapshot, `Ok(None)` when absent (no failed or\n/// in-flight sync), `Err` when present but unreadable / wrong\n/// version.\npub fn load(repo: &Path) -> Result<Option<SyncState>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let path = state_path(repo);\n    if !path.exists() {\n        return Ok(None);\n    }\n    let map = toml_load(&path)?;\n    let require = |k: &str| -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n        map.get(k)\n            .cloned()\n            .ok_or_else(|| format!(\"{}: missing key '{k}'\", path.display()).into())\n    };\n    let version: u32 = require(\"sync-state.version\")?.parse()?;\n    if version != STATE_FORMAT_VERSION {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"{}: unsupported sync-state version {version} (expected {STATE_FORMAT_VERSION})\",\n            path.display()\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(Some(SyncState {\n        version,\n        op_id: require(\"sync-state.op_id\")?,\n        bookmark: require(\"sync-state.bookmark\")?,\n        remote: require(\"sync-state.remote\")?,\n        started_at: require(\"sync-state.started_at\")?,\n    }))\n}\n\n/// Remove `repo`'s snapshot if present (success path / post-revert).\npub fn clear(repo: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let path = state_path(repo);\n    if path.exists() {\n        fs::remove_file(&path)?;\n        debug!(\"sync: cleared state at {}\", path.display());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::test_helpers::unique_base;\n\n    /// save → load round-trips every field; clear removes the file.\n    #[test]\n    fn save_load_clear_roundtrip() {\n        let repo = unique_base(\"sync-state\");\n        fs::create_dir_all(&repo).expect(\"mkdir fixture repo\");\n        save(&repo, \"abcdef123456\", \"main\", \"origin\").expect(\"save\");\n        let st = load(&repo).expect(\"load\").expect(\"state present\");\n        assert_eq!(st.version, STATE_FORMAT_VERSION);\n        assert_eq!(st.op_id, \"abcdef123456\");\n        assert_eq!(st.bookmark, \"main\");\n        assert_eq!(st.remote, \"origin\");\n        assert!(!st.started_at.is_empty());\n        clear(&repo).expect(\"clear\");\n        assert!(load(&repo).expect(\"load after clear\").is_none());\n        fs::remove_dir_all(&repo).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// Absent file loads as `None`.\n    #[test]\n    fn load_absent_is_none() {\n        let repo = unique_base(\"sync-state-absent\");\n        fs::create_dir_all(&repo).expect(\"mkdir fixture repo\");\n        assert!(load(&repo).expect(\"load\").is_none());\n        fs::remove_dir_all(&repo).ok();\n    }\n}\n"
}

243	///
244	/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,
245	/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for
246	/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo
247	/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic
248	/// "either it all went through or nothing changed" outcome.
249	///
250	/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or
251	/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence
252	/// under parallel `cargo test`.
253	pub fn sync_repos(
254	    repos: &[PathBuf],
255	    params: &SyncParams,
256	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
257	    debug!(
258	        "sync: enter (check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})",
259	        params.check, params.bookmark, params.remote
260	    );
261	
262	    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any
263	    // fetch/rebase. Implements "Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection"
264	    // — design at:
265	    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design
266	    for repo in repos {
267	        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &params.bookmark)?;
268	    }
269	
270	    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
271	    for repo in repos {
272	        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;
273	        debug!("{}: op snapshot = {op_id}", repo.display());
274	        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));
275	    }
276	
277	    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, params);
278	
279	    if let Err(e) = &result {
280	        warn!("sync failed: {e}");
281	        warn!("reverting all repos to starting state...");
282	        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {
283	            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {
284	                Ok(()) => info!("  {}: reverted to op {op_id}", repo.display()),
285	                Err(re) => warn!("  {}: revert failed: {re}", repo.display()),
286	            }
287	        }
288	        debug!("sync: exit");
289	        return result;
290	    }
291	
292	    // Reposition `@` onto the freshly-synced bookmark. Skipped in
293	    // deprecated verify-only mode, runs only after every repo synced
294	    // cleanly, and sits OUTSIDE the `op_restore` revert region above:
295	    // a reposition failure (e.g. the session repo's `@-` off main) is
296	    // surfaced without rolling back the successful fetch /
297	    // fast-forward.
298	    if !params.check {
299	        for (repo, _) in &snapshots {
300	            reposition_at(repo, &params.bookmark, params)?;
301	        }
302	    }
303	
304	    debug!("sync: exit");
305	    Ok(())
306	}

Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence\n/// under parallel `cargo test`.\npub fn sync_repos(\n    repos: &[PathBuf],\n    params: &SyncParams,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\n        \"sync: enter (check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        params.check, params.bookmark, params.remote\n    );\n\n    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any\n    // fetch/rebase. Implements \"Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection\"\n    // — design at:\n    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design\n    for repo in repos {\n        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &params.bookmark)?;\n    }\n\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, params);\n\n    if let Err(e) = &result {\n        warn!(\"sync failed: {e}\");\n        warn!(\"reverting all repos to starting state...\");\n        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {\n            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {\n                Ok(()) => info!(\"  {}: reverted to op {op_id}\", repo.display()),\n                Err(re) => warn!(\"  {}: revert failed: {re}\", repo.display()),\n            }\n        }\n        debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n        return result;\n    }\n\n    // Reposition `@` onto the freshly-synced bookmark. Skipped in\n    // deprecated verify-only mode, runs only after every repo synced\n    // cleanly, and sits OUTSIDE the `op_restore` revert region above:\n    // a reposition failure (e.g. the session repo's `@-` off main) is\n    // surfaced without rolling back the successful fetch /\n    // fast-forward.\n    if !params.check {\n        for (repo, _) in &snapshots {\n            reposition_at(repo, &params.bookmark, params)?;\n        }\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in dry-run).\n///\n/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can trigger the\n/// cross-repo revert; partial success across repos is *not* a valid\n/// end state for this command.",
  "new_string": "///\n/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,\n/// snapshot each repo's current op id (persisted to\n/// `.vc-x1/sync-state.toml` per repo), then hand off to `run_plan`\n/// for fetch + classify + act, then reposition `@`.\n///\n/// **Stop-on-error**: a failure leaves every repo exactly where the\n/// failing step stopped so the user can inspect what happened —\n/// nothing is auto-reverted. The error report names each repo's\n/// pre-sync op id and points at `vc-x1 revert`, which consumes the\n/// persisted snapshots. On full success the snapshots are cleared\n/// (a stale file must not become a revert target later).\n///\n/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or\n/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence\n/// under parallel `cargo test`.\npub fn sync_repos(\n    repos: &[PathBuf],\n    params: &SyncParams,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\n        \"sync: enter (check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        params.check, params.bookmark, params.remote\n    );\n\n    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any\n    // fetch/rebase. Implements \"Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection\"\n    // — design at:\n    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design\n    for repo in repos {\n        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &params.bookmark)?;\n    }\n\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        state::save(repo, &op_id, &params.bookmark, &params.remote)?;\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    // Run the plan, then reposition `@` onto the freshly-synced\n    // bookmark (skipped in deprecated verify-only mode). Both live\n    // inside the same stop-on-error region: any failure falls\n    // through to the report below with state left in place.\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, params).and_then(|()| {\n        if !params.check {\n            for (repo, _) in &snapshots {\n                reposition_at(repo, &params.bookmark, params)?;\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    });\n\n    if let Err(e) = &result {\n        warn!(\"sync failed: {e}\");\n        warn!(\"stopping — state left as-is for inspection (no auto-revert)\");\n        warn!(\"pre-sync op snapshot per repo:\");\n        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {\n            warn!(\"  {}: op {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        }\n        warn!(\"undo with `vc-x1 revert`, or per repo: jj op restore <op> -R <repo>\");\n        debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n        return result;\n    }\n\n    // Full success — the snapshots are no longer revert targets.\n    for (repo, _) in &snapshots {\n        state::clear(repo)?;\n    }\n\n    debug!(\"sync: exit\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in verify-only).\n///\n/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can stop and\n/// report; partial progress across repos is left in place for\n/// inspection (see `sync_repos`)."
}

1	//! The `sync` subcommand: fetch + classify + rebase / fast-forward
2	//! a workspace's repos against their remotes, then reposition `@`
3	//! onto the synced bookmark.
4	//!
5	//! Sync is a single atomic operation — verify-then-act happens
6	//! inside one invocation against one fetch snapshot (a separate
7	//! check-then-apply pair of runs would race the remote). The
8	//! hidden deprecated `--check` flag preserves the old verify-only
9	//! mode for `push`'s preflight until that is rewired in-process.
10	//!
11	//! **Stop-on-error**: a failure leaves state where the failing step
12	//! stopped so the user can inspect it. Each repo's pre-sync op id
13	//! is persisted to `.vc-x1/sync-state.toml` (see `state`); the
14	//! error report points at `vc-x1 revert` to undo explicitly.
15	//!
16	//! `current_op_id` / `op_restore` are `pub(crate)` so `push` (and
17	//! the `revert` subcommand) reuse the same snapshot-and-restore
18	//! primitives.
19	//!
20	//! `current_op_id` / `op_restore` are `pub(crate)` so `push` reuses
21	//! the same snapshot-and-restore pattern.
22	
23	use std::io::IsTerminal;
24	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
25	use std::process::Command;
26	
27	use clap::Args;
28	use log::{LevelFilter, debug, info, warn};
29	
30	use crate::common::{default_scope, find_workspace_root, prompt, run, scope_to_repos};
```

300	/// Scenario 2c: `@` has trailing writes and local+remote both
301	/// modify the same file differently. Rebase produces conflicts;
302	/// sync fails and op-restores both repos. The trailing content
303	/// must survive the revert — jj documents that op restore rolls
304	/// back operation history without clobbering the working copy.
305	#[test]
306	fn sync_conflict_preserves_trailing_at_on_revert() {
307	    let fx = Fixture::new("trailing-conflict");
308	    let remote_claude = fx.base.join("remote-claude.git");
309	    push_from_clone(
310	        &fx.base,
311	        &remote_claude,
312	        "claude2",
313	        "shared.txt",
314	        "remote-version\n",
315	        "feat: remote shared",
316	    );
317	    // Local commit on main with conflicting content
318	    add_local_commit(
319	        &fx.claude,
320	        "shared.txt",
321	        "local-version\n",
322	        "feat: local shared (conflicting)",
323	    );
324	    // Trailing writes on new @
325	    fs::write(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl"), "{\"line\":3}\n").expect("write trailing file");
326	
327	    let pre_main = cid(&fx.claude, "main");
328	    let pre_remote = cid(&fx.claude, "main@origin");
329	
330	    let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params())
331	        .unwrap_err()
332	        .to_string();
333	    assert!(
334	        err.contains("conflicts"),
335	        "expected conflict error, got: {err}"
336	    );
337	
338	    // Post-revert: main and main@origin back to pre-sync.
339	    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, "main"), pre_main, "main reverted");
340	    assert_eq!(
341	        cid(&fx.claude, "main@origin"),
342	        pre_remote,
343	        "main@origin reverted"
344	    );
345	    // No conflicts remain.
346	    let conflicts = jj(
347	        &fx.claude,
348	        &[
349	            "log",
350	            "-r",
351	            "conflicts()",
352	            "--no-graph",
353	            "-T",
354	            r#"commit_id ++ "\n""#,
355	        ],
356	    );
357	    assert!(
358	        conflicts.trim().is_empty(),
359	        "no conflicts should remain after revert"
360	    );
361	    // Trailing content preserved on disk.
362	    assert_eq!(
363	        fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl")).unwrap(),
364	        "{\"line\":3}\n",
365	        "trailing content preserved across op restore"
366	    );
367	}

  "new_string": "/// Scenario 2c: `@` has trailing writes and local+remote both\n/// modify the same file differently. Rebase produces conflicts;\n/// sync stops with the conflicted state left in place for\n/// inspection (no auto-revert) and the persisted snapshot still\n/// present as the manual revert target. Trailing content stays on\n/// disk — the rebase carries `@` along, it never rewrites the\n/// working-copy file.\n#[test]\nfn sync_conflict_stops_and_keeps_state() {\n    let fx = Fixture::new(\"trailing-conflict\");\n    let remote_claude = fx.base.join(\"remote-claude.git\");\n    push_from_clone(\n        &fx.base,\n        &remote_claude,\n        \"claude2\",\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"remote-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: remote shared\",\n    );\n    // Local commit on main with conflicting content\n    add_local_commit(\n        &fx.claude,\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"local-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: local shared (conflicting)\",\n    );\n    // Trailing writes on new @\n    fs::write(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\"), \"{\\\"line\\\":3}\\n\").expect(\"write trailing file\");\n\n    let pre_op = current_op_id(&fx.claude).expect(\"pre-sync op id\");\n\n    let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params())\n        .unwrap_err()\n        .to_string();\n    assert!(\n        err.contains(\"conflicts\"),\n        \"expected conflict error, got: {err}\"\n    );\n\n    // Stop-on-error: the conflicted state is still there to inspect.\n    assert!(\n        has(&fx.claude, \"conflicts()\"),\n        \"conflicted commits left in place for inspection\"\n    );\n    // The persisted snapshot names the pre-sync op (revert target).\n    let st = state::load(&fx.claude)\n        .expect(\"load sync state\")\n        .expect(\"state file present after failure\");\n    assert_eq!(st.op_id, pre_op, \"state records the pre-sync op id\");\n    // Trailing content preserved on disk.\n    assert_eq!(\n        fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join(\"trailing.jsonl\")).unwrap(),\n        \"{\\\"line\\\":3}\\n\",\n        \"trailing content preserved across the stop\"\n    );\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/sync/integration_tests.rs",
  "old_string": "#[test]\nfn sync_diverged_conflict_reverts() {\n    let fx = Fixture::new(\"conflict\");\n    let remote_code = fx.base.join(\"remote-code.git\");\n    push_from_clone(\n        &fx.base,\n        &remote_code,\n        \"work2\",\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"remote-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: remote shared\",\n    );\n    add_local_commit(\n        &fx.work,\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"local-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: local shared (conflicting)\",\n    );\n\n    let pre_main = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n    let pre_remote = cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\");\n\n    let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params())\n        .unwrap_err()\n        .to_string();\n    assert!(\n        err.contains(\"rebase produced conflicts\"),\n        \"unexpected error: {err}\"\n    );\n\n    // After revert: main and main@origin are back where they started.\n    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), pre_main, \"main should be reverted\");\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\"),\n        pre_remote,\n        \"main@origin should be reverted (pre-fetch state)\"\n    );\n    // No conflicts remain.\n    let conflicts = jj(\n        &fx.work,\n        &[\n            \"log\",\n            \"-r\",\n            \"conflicts()\",\n            \"--no-graph\",\n            \"-T\",\n            r#\"commit_id ++ \"\\n\"\"#,\n        ],\n    );\n    assert!(\n        conflicts.trim().is_empty(),\n        \"no conflicts should remain after revert\"\n    );\n}",
  "new_string": "#[test]\nfn sync_diverged_conflict_stops_and_keeps_state() {\n    let fx = Fixture::new(\"conflict\");\n    let remote_code = fx.base.join(\"remote-code.git\");\n    push_from_clone(\n        &fx.base,\n        &remote_code,\n        \"work2\",\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"remote-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: remote shared\",\n    );\n    add_local_commit(\n        &fx.work,\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"local-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: local shared (conflicting)\",\n    );\n\n    let pre_op_work = current_op_id(&fx.work).expect(\"work op id\");\n    let pre_op_claude = current_op_id(&fx.claude).expect(\"claude op id\");\n\n    let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params())\n        .unwrap_err()\n        .to_string();\n    assert!(\n        err.contains(\"rebase produced conflicts\"),\n        \"unexpected error: {err}\"\n    );\n\n    // Stop-on-error: the conflicted rebase result is left in place.\n    assert!(\n        has(&fx.work, \"conflicts()\"),\n        \"conflicted commits left in place for inspection\"\n    );\n    // Every repo's persisted snapshot survives as the revert target\n    // — including .claude, which synced cleanly before the failure.\n    let st_work = state::load(&fx.work)\n        .expect(\"load work sync state\")\n        .expect(\"work state present after failure\");\n    assert_eq!(st_work.op_id, pre_op_work, \"work state = pre-sync op\");\n    let st_claude = state::load(&fx.claude)\n        .expect(\"load claude sync state\")\n        .expect(\"claude state present after failure\");\n    assert_eq!(\n        st_claude.op_id, pre_op_claude,\n        \"claude state = pre-sync op\"\n    );\n    // Manual revert (what `vc-x1 revert` will drive in -4) restores\n    // the pre-sync state cleanly.\n    op_restore(&fx.work, &st_work.op_id).expect(\"manual op restore\");\n    assert!(\n        !has(&fx.work, \"conflicts()\"),\n        \"no conflicts after manual restore\"\n    );\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// Scenario 1: fresh fixture, nothing to do — `sync` leaves both\n/// repos untouched.\n#[test]\nfn sync_up_to_date() {\n    let fx = Fixture::new(\"up-to-date\");\n    let work_main = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n    let claude_main = cid(&fx.claude, \"main\");\n    sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), work_main);\n    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, \"main\"), claude_main);\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Scenario 1: fresh fixture, nothing to do — `sync` leaves both\n/// repos untouched and clears the persisted snapshots on success\n/// (a stale file must not become a later revert target).\n#[test]\nfn sync_up_to_date() {\n    let fx = Fixture::new(\"up-to-date\");\n    let work_main = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n    let claude_main = cid(&fx.claude, \"main\");\n    sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params()).expect(\"sync should succeed\");\n    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), work_main);\n    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, \"main\"), claude_main);\n    assert!(\n        state::load(&fx.work).expect(\"load work state\").is_none(),\n        \"state cleared on success (work)\"\n    );\n    assert!(\n        state::load(&fx.claude).expect(\"load claude state\").is_none(),\n        \"state cleared on success (claude)\"\n    );\n}"
}

> AGENT

`-3` is done and green (385 bin tests now, including the two new state round-trip tests). Work summary:

- **`src/sync/state.rs`** (new) — the persisted pre-sync snapshot, one file per synced repo at `<repo>/.vc-x1/sync-state.toml` (same gitignored dir as push's state): `save`/`load`/`clear` of `{version, op_id, bookmark, remote, started_at}`, flat-TOML in push's style. Per-repo rather than one combined file because sync accepts arbitrary repo lists. `load` carries an `#[allow(dead_code)]` with a note — its production caller is `-4`'s `revert`.
- **`src/sync.rs`** — the auto-revert block is gone. Snapshots are persisted before any fetch; `run_plan` + the reposition pass now share one stop-on-error region; on failure sync **leaves everything in place** and reports: the error, "no auto-revert", each repo's pre-sync op id, and both undo routes (`vc-x1 revert`, or manual `jj op restore <op> -R <repo>`). On full success the snapshots are cleared so a stale file can't become a revert target later. Docs updated (module, `sync_repos`, `run_plan`, `op_restore`).
- **`src/sync/integration_tests.rs`** — the two auto-revert tests are rewritten to the new contract: `sync_conflict_stops_and_keeps_state` and `sync_diverged_conflict_stops_and_keeps_state` assert conflicts *remain* for inspection, the state file records the pre-sync op id (for `.claude` too — the repo that synced cleanly before the failure), and a manual `op_restore` from that id cleans up (previewing what `revert` automates). `sync_up_to_date` additionally asserts success clears the state files.
- **`notes/todo.md`** marker, **`Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock`** → `0.67.0-3`, installed.

One behavior note worth your eyes: with stop-on-error, a *partial* failure across repos no longer rolls back the repos that already synced — the old "all or nothing" contract is replaced by "stopped where it failed, everything inspectable, revert restores all snapshots". That's the philosophy you asked for, applied cross-repo.

Ready to commit — please review.

1	//! `SubcommandRunner` trait
2	//!
3	//! The purpose is to simplify invoking a subcommand so `main`
4	//! can invoke any subcommand with one line:
5	//!
6	//! `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(&ctx),`
7	//!
8	//! Instead of the ~11 lines that the default `dispatch` below
9	//! now encapsulates.
10	//!
11	//! Implementors provide:
12	//!
13	//! - [`SubcommandRunner::to_params`] — build the clap-free
14	//!   `Params` from the clap `Args` (covers both `From` /
15	//!   `TryFrom` shapes uniformly via `Result<_, String>`).
16	//! - [`SubcommandRunner::run`] — the subcommand body.
17	//!
18	//! The default [`SubcommandRunner::dispatch`] builds `Params`
19	//! via `to_params`, runs via `run`, and maps the result to
20	//! `ExitCode`. Errors at any stage log via `error!` and return
21	//! `ExitCode::FAILURE`. `Context` is loaded once in `main` and
22	//! passed in by reference so a single `Context` is shared across
23	//! the (one) match arm that actually runs.
24	//!
25	//! ## See also
26	//!
27	//! - The wider CLI-args / subcommand-layer split this trait
28	//!   formalizes: [`ARCHITECTURE.md > args → Context + Params`][arch].
29	//! - Cycle design, ladder, evaluation gate:
30	//!   [`chores-10.md > 0.50.0-0`][open].
31	//! - Worked example, trait-shape decisions, naming:
32	//!   [`chores-10.md > 0.50.0-1`][port].
33	//! - First implementor: [`crate::chid`].
34	//!
35	//! [arch]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/ARCHITECTURE.md#args--context--params
36	//! [open]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-10.md#chore-open-subcommand-trait-sweep-0500-0
37	//! [port]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores/chores-10.md#refactor-subcommandrunner-trait--chid-0500-1
38	
39	use std::process::ExitCode;
40	
41	use log::error;
42	
43	use crate::context::Context;
44	
45	/// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so
46	/// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(&ctx)`
47	/// call.
48	pub trait SubcommandRunner {
49	    /// The clap-free `Params` struct the subcommand body
50	    /// consumes.
51	    type Params;
52	
53	    /// Build `Params` from `Args`. `Result<_, String>` absorbs
54	    /// both `From` (return `Ok(...)`) and `TryFrom` (forward
55	    /// the error) shapes uniformly.
56	    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;
57	
58	    /// Run the subcommand body.
59	    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;
60	
61	    /// Default dispatch: build `Params` via `to_params`, bracket
62	    /// the run with `crate::bm_track` enter/exit, execute via
63	    /// `run`, and map the result to `ExitCode`. Errors at any
64	    /// stage log via `error!` and return `ExitCode::FAILURE`.
65	    /// `bm_track` itself emits at `debug!`, so default runs stay
66	    /// quiet and the detached `finalize --exec` child (which runs
67	    /// without `-v`) silently no-ops — no per-call gate is needed
68	    /// here. `surface_previous_failures` for finalize's failure
69	    /// markers is handled in `main` before dispatch, so dispatch
70	    /// has no session-chrome responsibility at all anymore.
71	    fn dispatch(&self, ctx: &Context) -> ExitCode {
72	        let params = match self.to_params() {
73	            Ok(p) => p,
74	            Err(e) => {
75	                error!("{e}");
76	                return ExitCode::FAILURE;
77	            }
78	        };
79	
80	        // Command name is the first positional after the binary;
81	        // clap has already validated it by the time we reach
82	        // dispatch (top-level parse errors exit earlier).
83	        let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand
84	
85	        crate::bm_track("enter", &command_name);
86	        let exit_code = match Self::run(ctx, &params) {
87	            Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
88	            Err(e) => {
89	                error!("{e}");
90	                ExitCode::FAILURE
91	            }
92	        };
93	        crate::bm_track("exit ", &command_name);
94	        exit_code
95	    }
96	}
97	
```

  "old_string": "/// Resolve `params` into the concrete repo list `sync_repos`\n/// operates on.\n///\n/// `-R` and `--scope` compose:\n///\n/// - neither — workspace-default scope against the discovered root.\n/// - `-R PATH` alone — sync just that repo.\n/// - `-s ROLES` alone — roles against the discovered workspace root.\n/// - `-R PATH -s ROLES` — roles against `PATH` as the workspace root.\nfn resolve_params_to_repos(\n    params: &SyncParams,\n) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match (&params.repo, &params.scope) {\n        (None, None) => {\n            let root = find_workspace_root();\n            scope_to_repos(&default_scope(root.as_deref()), root.as_deref())\n        }\n        (Some(p), None) => Ok(vec![p.clone()]),\n        (None, Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, find_workspace_root().as_deref()),\n        (Some(p), Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, Some(p)),\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Resolve a `-R`/`--scope` pair into a concrete repo list.\n///\n/// `-R` and `--scope` compose:\n///\n/// - neither — workspace-default scope against the discovered root.\n/// - `-R PATH` alone — just that repo.\n/// - `-s ROLES` alone — roles against the discovered workspace root.\n/// - `-R PATH -s ROLES` — roles against `PATH` as the workspace root.\n///\n/// `pub(crate)` — shared by `sync` and `revert`, which must resolve\n/// the same invocation shape to the same repos.\npub(crate) fn resolve_repos(\n    repo: &Option<PathBuf>,\n    scope: &Option<Scope>,\n) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    match (repo, scope) {\n        (None, None) => {\n            let root = find_workspace_root();\n            scope_to_repos(&default_scope(root.as_deref()), root.as_deref())\n        }\n        (Some(p), None) => Ok(vec![p.clone()]),\n        (None, Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, find_workspace_root().as_deref()),\n        (Some(p), Some(s)) => scope_to_repos(s, Some(p)),\n    }\n}"
}

  "content": "//! The `revert` subcommand: restore repos to their persisted\n//! pre-sync snapshots.\n//!\n//! Sync stops on error and leaves state in place for inspection\n//! (see `sync`); each synced repo keeps its pre-sync `jj op` id in\n//! `.vc-x1/sync-state.toml`. `revert` is the explicit undo: it\n//! resolves the same `-R`/`--scope` invocation shape as sync,\n//! `jj op restore`s every repo that has a snapshot, and clears the\n//! consumed state files. Repos without a snapshot are skipped with\n//! a note; finding none at all is an error (\"nothing to revert\").\n\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse log::info;\n\nuse crate::context::Context;\nuse crate::options_flags::scope::{Scope, parse_scope};\nuse crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;\nuse crate::sync::state;\nuse crate::sync::{op_restore, resolve_repos};\n\n/// CLI args for `vc-x1 revert`.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct RevertArgs {\n    /// Workspace root, or a single jj repo to revert on its own.\n    ///\n    /// - `-R PATH` alone — revert just the repo at PATH.\n    /// - `-R PATH -s ROLES` — use PATH as the workspace root and\n    ///   revert the named side(s).\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long = \"repo\", value_name = \"PATH\", verbatim_doc_comment)]\n    pub repo: Option<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// Which repo(s) of the workspace to revert.\n    ///\n    /// `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot` — same resolution as `sync`, so a\n    /// failed `vc-x1 sync` and the following `vc-x1 revert` name\n    /// the same repos when invoked the same way.\n    #[arg(\n        short = 's',\n        long,\n        value_name = \"SCOPE\",\n        value_parser = parse_scope,\n        verbatim_doc_comment\n    )]\n    pub scope: Option<Scope>,\n}\n\n/// Inputs to the revert op, flat, owned, clap-free.\n///\n/// - `repo`: `-R/--repo` path (None ⇒ discover the workspace\n///   root from cwd).\n/// - `scope`: `--scope` parsed (None ⇒ workspace-default scope).\npub struct RevertParams {\n    pub repo: Option<PathBuf>,\n    pub scope: Option<Scope>,\n}\n\nimpl From<&RevertArgs> for RevertParams {\n    /// Convert clap-derived `RevertArgs` into the flat\n    /// `RevertParams` (total — every field copies straight over).\n    fn from(a: &RevertArgs) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            repo: a.repo.clone(),\n            scope: a.scope.clone(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl SubcommandRunner for RevertArgs {\n    type Params = RevertParams;\n\n    /// Delegate to the existing `From<&RevertArgs>` impl above\n    /// (total — never fails).\n    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String> {\n        Ok(RevertParams::from(self))\n    }\n\n    /// Run the revert op.\n    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n        revert(ctx, params)\n    }\n}\n\n/// CLI entry point for the `revert` subcommand.\n///\n/// Thin wrapper over `revert_repos` that resolves `-R`/`--scope`\n/// exactly the way `sync` does. `ctx` is unused today — present\n/// for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.\npub fn revert(_ctx: &Context, params: &RevertParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repos = resolve_repos(&params.repo, &params.scope)?;\n    revert_repos(&repos)\n}\n\n/// Restore every repo in `repos` that has a persisted pre-sync\n/// snapshot; clear each consumed state file.\n///\n/// - A repo without a snapshot is skipped with a note — sync\n///   clears state on success, so \"no snapshot\" is the normal\n///   post-success condition, not an error per repo.\n/// - No snapshot in *any* repo is an error: the user asked to\n///   revert something that isn't there.\n/// - A restore failure stops immediately (stop-on-error, like\n///   sync); already-restored repos keep their cleared state,\n///   remaining repos keep their snapshots for a re-run.\npub fn revert_repos(repos: &[PathBuf]) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut reverted = 0usize;\n    for repo in repos {\n        match state::load(repo)? {\n            Some(st) => {\n                info!(\n                    \"{}: jj op restore {} (pre-sync snapshot of '{}' @ {}, taken {})\",\n                    repo.display(),\n                    st.op_id,\n                    st.bookmark,\n                    st.remote,\n                    st.started_at\n                );\n                op_restore(repo, &st.op_id)?;\n                state::clear(repo)?;\n                reverted += 1;\n            }\n            None => {\n                info!(\"{}: no sync snapshot — skipping\", repo.display());\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    if reverted == 0 {\n        return Err(\"revert: no sync snapshots found — nothing to revert\".into());\n    }\n    let noun = if reverted == 1 { \"repo\" } else { \"repos\" };\n    info!(\"revert: {reverted} {noun} restored\");\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use crate::options_flags::scope::Side;\n    use clap::Parser;\n\n    #[derive(Parser)]\n    struct Cli {\n        #[command(flatten)]\n        args: RevertArgs,\n    }\n\n    /// Defaults: no `-R`, no `--scope` — workspace-default\n    /// resolution at run time.\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_defaults() {\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\"]).unwrap();\n        assert!(cli.args.repo.is_none());\n        assert!(cli.args.scope.is_none());\n    }\n\n    /// `-R PATH` and `-s SCOPE` parse and flow through to params.\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_repo_and_scope() {\n        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([\"test\", \"-R\", \"./solo\", \"-s\", \"code\"]).unwrap();\n        let params = RevertParams::from(&cli.args);\n        assert_eq!(params.repo, Some(PathBuf::from(\"./solo\")));\n        assert_eq!(params.scope, Some(Scope(vec![Side::Code])));\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

  "new_string": "/// Scenario 5b: `vc-x1 revert` after a failed sync — the full\n/// inspect-then-undo loop. Same conflict fixture as scenario 5;\n/// `revert_repos` restores every repo from its persisted snapshot\n/// (including `.claude`, which synced cleanly before the failure),\n/// clears the consumed state files, and a second revert errors\n/// (\"nothing to revert\").\n#[test]\nfn revert_restores_after_failed_sync() {\n    let fx = Fixture::new(\"revert-after-conflict\");\n    let remote_code = fx.base.join(\"remote-code.git\");\n    push_from_clone(\n        &fx.base,\n        &remote_code,\n        \"work2\",\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"remote-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: remote shared\",\n    );\n    add_local_commit(\n        &fx.work,\n        \"shared.txt\",\n        \"local-version\\n\",\n        \"feat: local shared (conflicting)\",\n    );\n\n    let pre_main = cid(&fx.work, \"main\");\n    let pre_remote = cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\");\n\n    sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &default_params()).expect_err(\"sync should fail on conflicts\");\n    assert!(has(&fx.work, \"conflicts()\"), \"conflicted state to undo\");\n\n    crate::revert::revert_repos(&fx.repos()).expect(\"revert should succeed\");\n\n    // Pre-sync state is back: bookmark, remote-tracking, no conflicts.\n    assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, \"main\"), pre_main, \"main restored\");\n    assert_eq!(\n        cid(&fx.work, \"main@origin\"),\n        pre_remote,\n        \"main@origin restored (pre-fetch state)\"\n    );\n    assert!(!has(&fx.work, \"conflicts()\"), \"no conflicts after revert\");\n    // Consumed snapshots are cleared in both repos.\n    assert!(\n        state::load(&fx.work).expect(\"load work state\").is_none(),\n        \"work state cleared by revert\"\n    );\n    assert!(\n        state::load(&fx.claude).expect(\"load claude state\").is_none(),\n        \"claude state cleared by revert\"\n    );\n    // Nothing left to revert — explicit error, not a silent no-op.\n    let err = crate::revert::revert_repos(&fx.repos())\n        .unwrap_err()\n        .to_string();\n    assert!(\n        err.contains(\"nothing to revert\"),\n        \"expected nothing-to-revert error, got: {err}\"\n    );\n}\n\n/// Scenario 6: code repo behind with a clean `@`. Fetch fast-forwards\n/// main; reposition then `jj new`s the empty `@` onto the new tip."
}

> AGENT

`0.67.0-4` is complete — `vc-x1 revert` exists and the whole inspect-then-undo loop is covered end-to-end. Work summary:

- **`src/revert.rs`** (new) — the `revert` subcommand on the standard `SubcommandRunner` pattern. `-R`/`--scope` resolve exactly like sync (shared resolver, below), so a failed `vc-x1 sync` and the following `vc-x1 revert` name the same repos. Per repo with a snapshot: log the op id + what/when it was syncing, `jj op restore`, clear the state file. Snapshot-less repos are skipped with a note (normal post-success condition); zero snapshots anywhere is an error — as the smoke test above shows. A mid-way restore failure stops (same philosophy), leaving remaining snapshots for a re-run.
- **`src/sync.rs`** — `resolve_params_to_repos` generalized to `pub(crate) resolve_repos(repo, scope)` shared by both subcommands.
- **`src/sync/state.rs`** — the two `#[allow(dead_code)]` bridges removed now that `revert` is the production reader.
- **`src/main.rs`** — `revert` registered (module, `Commands::Revert` with long_about, dispatch arm); also fixed sync's `long_about`, which I'd left describing the old auto-revert in `-3` — it now states stop-on-error + `vc-x1 revert`.
- **`src/sync/integration_tests.rs`** — new `revert_restores_after_failed_sync`: conflict fixture → sync fails → `revert_repos` restores both repos (including `.claude`, which had synced cleanly), `main`/`main@origin` back to pre-sync, no conflicts, state cleared, and a second revert errors with "nothing to revert".
- Parse unit tests in `revert.rs`; version `0.67.0-4` installed.

388 bin tests + all CLI suites green. Ready to commit — please review.
